### Basic Socket.IO Test Setup and Event Emission

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Sets up a Socket.IO server and client for testing. This example demonstrates emitting a 'hello' event from the server and expecting it on the client with a specific payload.

```typescript
import { beforeAll, afterAll, describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { type AddressInfo } from "node:net";
import { io as ioc, type Socket as ClientSocket } from "socket.io-client";
import { Server, type Socket as ServerSocket } from "socket.io";

function waitFor(socket: ServerSocket | ClientSocket, event: string) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

describe("my awesome project", () => {
  let io: Server, serverSocket: ServerSocket, clientSocket: ClientSocket;

  beforeAll(() => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      const httpServer = createServer();
      io = new Server(httpServer);
      httpServer.listen(() => {
        const port = (httpServer.address() as AddressInfo).port;
        clientSocket = ioc(`http://localhost:${port}`);
        io.on("connection", (socket) => {
          serverSocket = socket;
        });
        clientSocket.on("connect", resolve);
      });
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.disconnect();
  });

  it("should work", () => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("world");
        resolve();
      });
      serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
    });
  });

  it("should work with an acknowledgement", () => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
        cb("hola");
      });
      clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("hola");
        resolve();
      });
    });
  });

  it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
      cb("bar");
    });
    const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");
    expect(result).toEqual("bar");
  });

  it("should work with waitFor()", () => {
    clientSocket.emit("baz");

    return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
  });
});

```

--------------------------------

### Starting Socket.IO Server (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/migrating-from-0-9

Shows the updated method for initializing and starting a Socket.IO server. The `io.listen()` method is deprecated, and server initialization is now done directly by passing options to the `io()` constructor.

```javascript
var io = require('socket.io');
var socket = io.listen(80, { /* options */ });
```

```javascript
var io = require('socket.io');
var socket = io({ /* options */ });
```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO Server Setup and Communication in Node.js

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3

Provides an example of setting up a Socket.IO server using Node.js and the socket.io library. It demonstrates listening for connections, sending messages using 'send' and 'emit', and handling incoming messages.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(3000);

io.on("connection", socket => {
  // either with send()
  socket.send("Hello!");

  // or with emit() and custom event names
  socket.emit("greetings", "Hey!", { "ms": "jane" }, Buffer.from([4, 3, 3, 1]));

  // handle the event sent with socket.send()
  socket.on("message", (data) => {
    console.log(data);
  });

  // handle the event sent with socket.emit()
  socket.on("salutations", (elem1, elem2, elem3) => {
    console.log(elem1, elem2, elem3);
  });
});

```

### Setup and Basic Test (ES Modules)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Sets up a Socket.IO server and client for testing using Jest and ES modules. Includes a basic test for event emission and a helper function 'waitFor'.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { io as ioc } from "socket.io-client";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

function waitFor(socket, event) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

describe("my awesome project", () => {
  let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;

  beforeAll((done) => {
    const httpServer = createServer();
    io = new Server(httpServer);
    httpServer.listen(() => {
      const port = httpServer.address().port;
      clientSocket = ioc(`http://localhost:${port}`);
      io.on("connection", (socket) => {
        serverSocket = socket;
      });
      clientSocket.on("connect", done);
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.disconnect();
  });

  test("should work", (done) => {
    clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
      expect(arg).toBe("world");
      done();
    });
    serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
  });

  test("should work with an acknowledgement", (done) => {
    serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
      cb("hola");
    });
    clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
      expect(arg).toBe("hola");
      done();
    });
  });

  test("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
      cb("bar");
    });
    const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");
    expect(result).toBe("bar");
  });

  test("should work with waitFor()", () => {
    clientSocket.emit("baz");

    return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
  });
});

```

### Setup Socket.IO Server and Client for Testing (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Initializes a Socket.IO server and a client instance for testing purposes. It creates an HTTP server, attaches the Socket.IO server, and establishes a client connection. The `serverSocket` is captured upon client connection.

```javascript
const test = require("tape");
const { createServer } = require("node:http");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");

  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

test("setup", (t) => {
  const httpServer = createServer();
  io = new Server(httpServer);
  httpServer.listen(() => {
    const port = httpServer.address().port;
    clientSocket = ioc(`http://localhost:${port}`);
    io.on("connection", (socket) => {
      serverSocket = socket;
    });
    clientSocket.on("connect", t.end);
  });
});
```

### Socket.IO Server and Client Setup (ES Modules)

  });
}

        io.on("connection", (socket) => {
          serverSocket = socket;
        });
        clientSocket.on("connect", resolve);
      });
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.disconnect();
  });

  it("should work", () => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("world");
        resolve();
      });
      serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
    });
  });

      serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
        cb("hola");
      });
      clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("hola");
        resolve();
      });
    });
  });

  it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
      cb("bar");
    });

```

### Socket.IO Server and Client Setup (CommonJS)

          serverSocket = socket;

        clientSocket.on("connect", resolve);
      });
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.disconnect();
  });

  it("should work", () => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("world");
        resolve();
      });
      serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
    });
  });

  it("should work with an acknowledgement", () => {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
        cb("hola");
      });
      clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
        expect(arg).toEqual("hola");
        resolve();
      });
    });
  });

  it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
      cb("bar");
    });
    const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");
    expect(result).toEqual("bar");
  });

  it("should work with waitFor()", () => {
    clientSocket.emit("baz");

    return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
  });
});

```

### Test Socket.IO with Tape

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/testing

This example demonstrates testing Socket.IO applications using the Tape testing framework. It includes setup for the Socket.IO server and client, and tests for message passing and acknowledgements. Tape provides a simpler assertion API compared to Chai or Jest. Dependencies include tape.

```javascript
const { createServer } = require("http");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");
const Client = require("socket.io-client");
const test = require("tape");

let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;

test("setup", (t) => {
  const httpServer = createServer();
  io = new Server(httpServer);
  httpServer.listen(() => {
    const port = httpServer.address().port;
    io.on("connection", (socket) => {
      serverSocket = socket;
    });
    clientSocket = new Client(`http://localhost:${port}`);
    clientSocket.on("connect", t.end);
  });
});

test("it works", (t) => {
  t.plan(1);
  clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
    t.equal(arg, "world");
  });
  serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
});

test("it works (with ack)", (t) => {
  t.plan(1);
  serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
    cb("hola");
  });
  clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
    t.equal(arg, "hola");
  });
});

test.onFinish(() => {
  io.close();
  clientSocket.close();
});
```

### Migrate socket.io-emitter to @socket.io/redis-emitter (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Demonstrates the migration from the legacy `socket.io-emitter` to the modern `@socket.io/redis-emitter` package. This involves initializing the emitter with a pre-configured Redis client, as the new package no longer manages client creation. Ensure you have the `redis` package installed for this example.

```javascript
const { Emitter } = require("@socket.io/redis-emitter");
const { createClient } = require("redis");

const redisClient = createClient();
const io = new Emitter(redisClient);

```

### Node.js Cluster Setup with @socket.io/sticky

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/using-multiple-nodes

This example demonstrates setting up Node.js cluster with the '@socket.io/sticky' package for sticky sessions. It configures both the master and worker processes, initializes Socket.IO with a cluster adapter, and handles worker exits.

```javascript
const cluster = require("cluster");
const http = require("http");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");
const numCPUs = require("os").cpus().length;
const { setupMaster, setupWorker } = require("@socket.io/sticky");
const { createAdapter, setupPrimary } = require("@socket.io/cluster-adapter");

if (cluster.isMaster) {
  console.log(`Master ${process.pid} is running`);

  const httpServer = http.createServer();

  // setup sticky sessions
  setupMaster(httpServer, {
    loadBalancingMethod: "least-connection",
  });

  // setup connections between the workers
  setupPrimary();

  // needed for packets containing buffers (you can ignore it if you only send plaintext objects)
  // Node.js < 16.0.0
  cluster.setupMaster({
    serialization: "advanced",
  });
  // Node.js > 16.0.0
  // cluster.setupPrimary({
  //   serialization: "advanced",
  // });

  httpServer.listen(3000);

  for (let i = 0; i < numCPUs; i++) {
    cluster.fork();
  }

  cluster.on("exit", (worker) => {
    console.log(`Worker ${worker.process.pid} died`);
    cluster.fork();
  });
} else {
  console.log(`Worker ${process.pid} started`);

  const httpServer = http.createServer();
  const io = new Server(httpServer);

  // use the cluster adapter
  io.adapter(createAdapter());

  // setup connection with the primary process
  setupWorker(io);

  io.on("connection", (socket) => {
    /* ... */
  });
}

```

### Initialize Socket.IO Redis Emitter (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Shows how to install and use the '@socket.io/redis-emitter' package in Node.js to send messages to connected Socket.IO clients from a separate process. It includes examples for different Redis client versions.

```javascript
import { Emitter } from "@socket.io/redis-emitter";
import { createClient } from "redis";

const redisClient = createClient({ url: "redis://localhost:6379" });

redisClient.connect().then(() => {
  const emitter = new Emitter(redisClient);

  setInterval(() => {
    emitter.emit("time", new Date);
  }, 5000);
});
```

```javascript
import { Emitter } from "@socket.io/redis-emitter";
import { createClient } from "redis";

const redisClient = createClient({ url: "redis://localhost:6379" });
const emitter = new Emitter(redisClient);

setInterval(() => {
  emitter.emit("time", new Date);
}, 5000);
```

### Socket.IO Server Initialization with TypeScript

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Demonstrates initializing a Socket.IO server using TypeScript. This setup leverages the new TypeScript codebase, eliminating the need for separate type definitions. It includes basic connection and disconnection event handling.

```typescript
import { Server, Socket } from "socket.io";  
  
const io = new Server(8080);  
  
io.on("connection", (socket: Socket) => {  
    console.log(`connect ${socket.id}`);  
  
    socket.on("disconnect", () => {  
        console.log(`disconnect ${socket.id}`);  
    });  
});  
```

### Server Initialization Examples (2.x)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-initialization

This section provides code examples for initializing a Socket.IO server using different JavaScript module systems (CommonJS, ES Modules, TypeScript) and integration scenarios (standalone, with existing HTTP server, Express, Koa).

```APIDOC
## Server Initialization

This section covers various ways to initialize the Socket.IO server.

### Standalone Initialization

Initializes Socket.IO as a standalone server, implicitly starting an HTTP server.

#### CommonJS
```javascript
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require('socket.io')(options);

io.on('connection', socket => { /* ... */ });

io.listen(3000);
```

#### Standalone with Port

You can also pass the port as the first argument.

```javascript
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require('socket.io')(3000, options);

io.on('connection', socket => { /* ... */ });
```

### Attached to an Existing HTTP Server

Attaches Socket.IO to an already running HTTP server.

#### CommonJS with HTTP
```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  // ...
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);
```

#### CommonJS with HTTPS
```javascript
const fs = require('fs');
const server = require('https').createServer({
  key: fs.readFileSync('/tmp/key.pem'),
  cert: fs.readFileSync('/tmp/cert.pem')
});
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require('socket.io')(server, options);

io.on('connection', socket => { /* ... */ });

server.listen(3000);
```

### With Express

Integrates Socket.IO with an Express application.

#### CommonJS
```javascript
const app = require('express')();
const server = require('http').createServer(app);
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require('socket.io')(server, options);

io.on('connection', socket => { /* ... */ });

server.listen(3000);
```

### With Koa

Integrates Socket.IO with a Koa application.

#### CommonJS
```javascript
const app = require('koa')();
const server = require('http').createServer(app.callback());
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require('socket.io')(server, options);

io.on('connection', socket => { /* ... */ });

server.listen(3000);
```

### ES Modules Initialization

Initializes Socket.IO using ES module syntax.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http";
import Server from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer();
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  // ...
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);
```

### TypeScript Initialization

Initializes Socket.IO using TypeScript.

```typescript
import { createServer } from "http";
import Server = require("socket.io");
import { Socket } from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer();
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  // ...
});

io.on("connection", (socket: Socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);
```
```

### Integrate Socket.IO with Fastify

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

Provides instructions and code examples for integrating Socket.IO with the Fastify framework using the `fastify-socket.io` plugin. It shows how to register the plugin, define routes, and access the Socket.IO server instance via `server.io` after the server is ready. Examples are in CommonJS, ES modules, and TypeScript.

```javascript
const fastify = require("fastify");
const fastifyIO = require("fastify-socket.io");

const server = fastify();
server.register(fastifyIO);

server.get("/", (req, reply) => {
  server.io.emit("hello");
});

server.ready().then(() => {
  // we need to wait for the server to be ready, else `server.io` is undefined
  server.io.on("connection", (socket) => {
    // ...
  });
});

server.listen({ port: 3000 });

```

### Integrate Socket.IO with µWebSockets.js Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-installation

Demonstrates how to bind a Socket.IO server to a µWebSockets.js application. This setup leverages the performance benefits of µWebSockets.js for Socket.IO connections.

```javascript
const { App } = require("uWebSockets.js");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");

const app = App();
const io = new Server();

io.attachApp(app);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

app.listen(3000, (token) => {
  if (!token) {
    console.warn("port already in use");
  }
});
```

### Setup Socket.IO with Hono in Node.js (TypeScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

This TypeScript snippet demonstrates integrating Socket.IO with Hono. It requires 'hono', '@hono/node-server', and 'socket.io', along with Node.js http types. The setup includes creating a Hono app, starting an HTTP server, and initializing Socket.IO, with explicit type casting for the HTTP server.

```typescript
import { Hono } from "hono";  
import { serve } from "@hono/node-server";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import type { Server as HTTPServer } from "node:http";  
  
const app = new Hono();  
  
const httpServer = serve({
    fetch: app.fetch,  
    port: 3000,  
});  
  
const io = new Server(httpServer as HTTPServer, {  
    /* options */  
});  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
    // ...  
});  
  


```

### Node.js Socket.IO Server Setup

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2

Sets up a basic Node.js HTTP server and integrates Socket.IO. It serves an 'index.html' file and listens for client connections, logging a message when a client connects.

```javascript
const content = require('fs').readFileSync(__dirname + '/index.html', 'utf8');

const httpServer = require('http').createServer((req, res) => {
  // serve the index.html file
  res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'text/html');
  res.setHeader('Content-Length', Buffer.byteLength(content));
  res.end(content);
});

const io = require('socket.io')(httpServer);

io.on('connection', socket => {
  console.log('connect');
});

httpServer.listen(3000, () => {
  console.log('go to http://localhost:3000');
});
```

### Client Authentication Setup

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

```APIDOC
## Client Authentication Setup

### Description
Configure authentication credentials, such as a token, to be sent with every connection request to a Socket.IO namespace. This is crucial for verifying client identity on the server.

### Method
Client-side initialization

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **auth** (object) - Required - An object containing authentication credentials.
  - **token** (string) - Required - The authentication token to be sent.

### Request Example
```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  auth: {
    token: "abcd"
  }
});
```

### Socket.IO Server-Side Implementation

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2

Provides an example of a Socket.IO server implementation using Node.js. It details how to set up a server on a specific port, handle incoming client connections, send messages using 'send()' and 'emit()', and listen for incoming messages and custom events.

```javascript
const io = require('socket.io')(3000);

io.on('connection', socket => {
  // either with send()
  socket.send('Hello!');

  // or with emit() and custom event names
  socket.emit('greetings', 'Hey!', { 'ms': 'jane' }, Buffer.from([4, 3, 3, 1]));

  // handle the event sent with socket.send()
  socket.on('message', (data) => {
    console.log(data);
  });

  // handle the event sent with socket.emit()
  socket.on('salutations', (elem1, elem2, elem3) => {
    console.log(elem1, elem2, elem3);
  });
});
```

  // options

  // ...

### Configure Caddy 2 for Socket.IO Proxying

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/reverse-proxy

These Caddy 2 configurations demonstrate how to set up reverse proxying for Socket.IO. The first example forwards all requests under `/socket.io/*` to the backend. The second example shows how to handle a custom path, including rewriting and proxying requests.

```caddy
example.com {
    reverse_proxy /socket.io/* localhost:3000
}
```

```caddy
example.com {
  rewrite /path /path/
  handle_path /path/* {
    rewrite * /socket.io{path}
    reverse_proxy localhost:3000
  }
}
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Demonstrates initializing a Socket.IO server with various configuration options. This includes setting a custom path for socket connections and disabling client file serving. It also shows how to attach the server to an existing HTTP server or directly listen on a port, with options for ping intervals and timeouts.

```javascript
const io = require('socket.io')({
  path: '/test',
  serveClient: false,
});

// either
const server = require('http').createServer();

io.attach(server, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});

server.listen(3000);

// or
io.attach(3000, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```

### Test Socket.IO with Mocha

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/testing

This example demonstrates how to test Socket.IO connections and message passing using Mocha and Chai. It sets up an HTTP server, a Socket.IO server instance, and a Socket.IO client instance. It tests basic message emission and acknowledgement patterns. Dependencies include mocha and chai.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http";
import { io as Client } from "socket.io-client";
import { Server } from "socket.io";
import { assert } from "chai";

describe("my awesome project", () => {
  let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;

  before((done) => {
    const httpServer = createServer();
    io = new Server(httpServer);
    httpServer.listen(() => {
      const port = httpServer.address().port;
      io.on("connection", (socket) => {
        serverSocket = socket;
      });
      clientSocket = new Client(`http://localhost:${port}`);
      clientSocket.on("connect", done);
    });
  });

  after(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.close();
  });

  it("should work", (done) => {
    clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
      assert.equal(arg, "world");
      done();
    });
    serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
  });

  it("should work (with ack)", (done) => {
    serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
      cb("hola");
    });
    clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
      assert.equal(arg, "hola");
      done();
    });
  });
});
```

### Socket.IO Server Initialization with Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-initialization

Demonstrates how to initialize a Socket.IO server with various configuration options.

```APIDOC
## Socket.IO Server Options

### Description
This section outlines the available options for configuring a Socket.IO server instance. These options allow for customization of adapter, parser, timeouts, buffer sizes, and transport behaviors.

### Options

#### `adapter`
- **Type**: Class
- **Default**: `socket.io-adapter` (in-memory adapter)
- **Description**: Specifies the adapter to use for broadcasting messages across Socket.IO instances. The `socket.io-redis` package is a common alternative.

#### `parser`
- **Type**: Class
- **Default**: `socket.io-parser`
- **Description**: The parser to use for serializing and deserializing messages.

#### `connectTimeout`
- **Type**: Number
- **Default**: `45000` (milliseconds)
- **Description**: The time limit before disconnecting a client that has not successfully joined a namespace.

#### `pingTimeout`
- **Type**: Number
- **Default**: `5000` (milliseconds)
- **Description**: The interval for sending pings to clients. If a pong is not received within this time, the server disconnects the client with the reason `ping timeout`.

#### `pingInterval`
- **Type**: Number
- **Default**: `25000` (milliseconds)
- **Description**: The interval between pings sent by the server. If the client does not receive a ping within `pingInterval + pingTimeout`, it disconnects.

#### `upgradeTimeout`
- **Type**: Number
- **Default**: `10000` (milliseconds)
- **Description**: The delay before cancelling an uncompleted transport upgrade.

#### `maxHttpBufferSize`
- **Type**: Number
- **Default**: `1e6` (1MB)
- **Description**: The maximum size in bytes allowed for a single message before closing the socket. This value can be adjusted based on application needs.

#### `allowRequest`
- **Type**: Function
- **Default**: `-`
- **Description**: A function that receives the handshake or upgrade request. It can be used to conditionally allow or deny the connection based on custom logic. The callback should be invoked with `callback(null, isAllowed)`.

#### `transports`
- **Type**: Array<String>
- **Default**: `["polling", "websocket"]`
- **Description**: An array of allowed low-level transports on the server-side.

#### `allowUpgrades`
- **Type**: Boolean
- **Default**: `true`
- **Description**: Controls whether transport upgrades are permitted.

#### `perMessageDeflate`
- **Type**: Boolean or Object
- **Default**: `false`
- **Description**: Enables the permessage-deflate extension for the WebSocket transport. If `false`, the `compress` flag on `emit` will be ignored for WebSockets. When set to an object, it allows configuration of options like `threshold`, `zlibDeflateOptions`, `zlibInflateOptions`, `clientNoContextTakeover`, `serverNoContextTakeover`, `serverMaxWindowBits`, and `concurrencyLimit`.

#### `httpCompression`
- **Type**: Boolean or Object
- **Default**: `true`
- **Description**: Enables compression for the HTTP long-polling transport. If `false`, the `compress` flag on `emit` will be ignored for HTTP long-polling. When set to an object, it allows configuration of options like `threshold`, `chunkSize`, `windowBits`, and `memLevel`.

### Examples

#### Using Redis Adapter
```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const redisClient = require("redis").createClient();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  adapter: require("socket.io-redis")({
    pubClient: redisClient,
    subClient: redisClient.duplicate()
  })
});
```

#### Adjusting `pingTimeout`
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  pingTimeout: 30000
});
```

#### Adjusting `maxHttpBufferSize`
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  maxHttpBufferSize: 1e8
});
```

#### Custom `allowRequest` Function
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  allowRequest: (req, callback) => {
    const isOriginValid = check(req);
    callback(null, isOriginValid);
  }
});
```

#### Configuring `perMessageDeflate`
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  perMessageDeflate: {
    threshold: 2048,
    zlibDeflateOptions: {
      chunkSize: 8 * 1024
    },
    zlibInflateOptions: {
      windowBits: 14,
      memLevel: 7
    },
    clientNoContextTakeover: true,
    serverNoContextTakeover: true,
    serverMaxWindowBits: 10,
    concurrencyLimit: 20
  }
});
```

#### Configuring `httpCompression`
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  httpCompression: {
    threshold: 2048,
    chunkSize: 8 * 1024,
    windowBits: 14,
    memLevel: 7
  }
});
```
```

--------------------------------

### Test Socket.IO with Jest

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/testing

This example shows how to test Socket.IO functionality using Jest. It sets up the necessary server and client instances for Socket.IO communication. The tests verify message emission and acknowledgement patterns, similar to the Mocha example. Dependencies include jest.

```javascript
const { createServer } = require("http");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");
const Client = require("socket.io-client");

describe("my awesome project", () => {
  let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;

  beforeAll((done) => {
    const httpServer = createServer();
    io = new Server(httpServer);
    httpServer.listen(() => {
      const port = httpServer.address().port;
      io.on("connection", (socket) => {
        serverSocket = socket;
      });
      clientSocket = new Client(`http://localhost:${port}`);
      clientSocket.on("connect", done);
    });
  });

  afterAll(() => {
    io.close();
    clientSocket.close();
  });

  test("should work", (done) => {
    clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {
      expect(arg).toBe("world");
      done();
    });
    serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");
  });

  test("should work (with ack)", (done) => {
    serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {
      cb("hola");
    });
    clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {
      expect(arg).toBe("hola");
      done();
    });
  });
});
```

### Create Basic Express Server (ES Modules)

```javascript

const app = express();
const server = createServer(app);

app.get('/', (req, res) => {
  res.send('<h1>Hello world</h1>');

  console.log('server running at http://localhost:3000');
});
```

### Webpack 5 Client Configuration for Browsers

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-with-bundlers

Configures Webpack 5 to bundle the socket.io client for browser environments. It includes installation instructions for necessary development dependencies and a `webpack.config.js` file. This setup uses babel-loader for transpilation and webpack-remove-debug to strip debug statements.

```bash
npm i -D socket.io-client webpack webpack-cli babel-loader @babel/core @babel/preset-env \\
    @babel/plugin-transform-object-assign webpack-remove-debug  
```

```javascript
module.exports = {
  entry: "./index.js",
  output: {
    filename: "bundle.js",
  },
  mode: "production",
  node: false,
  module: {
    rules: [
      {
        test: /\.m?js$/,
        use: {
          loader: "babel-loader",
          options: {
            presets: ["@babel/preset-env"], // ensure compatibility with older browsers
            plugins: ["@babel/plugin-transform-object-assign"], // ensure compatibility with IE 11
          },
        },
      },
      {
        test: /\.js$/,
        loader: "webpack-remove-debug", // remove "debug" package
      },
    ],
  },
};

```

### Socket.IO Test Suite with Mocha and Chai (ES Modules)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

This test suite utilizes ES modules for importing dependencies, demonstrating modern JavaScript practices for testing Socket.IO applications. It mirrors the functionality of the CommonJS version, including server setup, client connection, and event testing with acknowledgements.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "node:http";  
import { io as ioc } from "socket.io-client";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { assert } from "chai";  
  
function waitFor(socket, event) {  
  return new Promise((resolve) => {  
    socket.once(event, resolve);  
  });  
}  
  
describe("my awesome project", () => {  
  let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;  
  
  before((done) => {  
    const httpServer = createServer();  
    io = new Server(httpServer);  
    httpServer.listen(() => {  
      const port = httpServer.address().port;  
      clientSocket = ioc(`http://localhost:${port}`);  
      io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
        serverSocket = socket;  
      });  
      clientSocket.on("connect", done);  
    });  
  });  
  
  after(() => {  
    io.close();  
    clientSocket.disconnect();  
  });  
  
  it("should work", (done) => {  
    clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {  
      assert.equal(arg, "world");  
      done();  
    });  
    serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");  
  });  
  
  it("should work with an acknowledgement", (done) => {  
    serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {  
      cb("hola");  
    });  
    clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {  
      assert.equal(arg, "hola");  
      done();  
    });  
  });  
  
  it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {  
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {  
      cb("bar");  
    });  
    const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");  
    assert.equal(result, "bar");  
  });  
  
  it("should work with waitFor()", () => {  
    clientSocket.emit("baz");  
  
    return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");  
  });  
});  

```

### Get All Sockets in Namespace (Promise-based)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Demonstrates how to retrieve all connected socket IDs within a namespace using the `allSockets()` method, which returns a Promise. This replaces the older `clients()` method and can be used for the default namespace, specific namespaces, or even filtered by room.

```javascript
// all sockets in default namespace  
const ids = await io.allSockets();  

// all sockets in the "chat" namespace  
const ids = await io.of("/chat").allSockets();  

// all sockets in the "chat" namespace and in the "general" room  
const ids = await io.of("/chat").in("general").allSockets();  
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server Instance

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Demonstrates two ways to initialize a Socket.IO server instance. The first method uses `require('socket.io')()` directly, while the second uses named import with `new Server()` from `socket.io`. Both approaches are valid for setting up the server.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")();
// or
const { Server } = require("socket.io");
const io = new Server();
```

### Socket.IO Protocol Overhead Example

Source: https://socket.io/docs/index

Shows an example of how a simple Socket.IO message is transmitted over a WebSocket. It breaks down the packet structure, indicating the Engine.IO and Socket.IO packet types and the JSON-stringified arguments.

```javascript
socket.emit("hello", "world") will be sent as a single WebSocket frame containing 42["hello","world"] with:
* 4 being Engine.IO "message" packet type
* 2 being Socket.IO "message" packet type
* ["hello","world"] being the JSON.stringify()-ed version of the arguments array
```

### Creating and Using a Custom Namespace (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Demonstrates the server-side setup for a custom Socket.IO namespace '/my-namespace'. It shows how to establish a connection handler, log connections, and emit messages to clients within that specific namespace.

```javascript
const nsp = io.of("/my-namespace");

sp.on("connection", socket => {
  console.log("someone connected");
});

sp.emit("hi", "everyone!");
```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO v4: Correct Room and Broadcast Emission Syntax

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

Demonstrates the correct way to emit messages to specific rooms or broadcast to all clients in Socket.IO v4. The `broadcast` flag is redundant when using `to()` for room targeting. This addresses the 'cannot get emit of undefined' error.

```javascript
socket.broadcast.emit(/* ... */); // to all clients but the sender
socket.to("room1").emit(/* ... */); // to clients in "room1" but the sender

// VALID (but useless 'broadcast' flag)
socket.broadcast.to("room1").emit(/* ... */);

// INVALID
socket.to("room1").broadcast.emit(/* ... */);
```

### Client Initialization for Namespaces (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/namespaces

Provides examples of how to initialize Socket.IO client connections to specific namespaces, both for same-origin and cross-origin scenarios.

```javascript
// Same-origin version
const socket = io(); // Connects to the main namespace "/"
const mainNamespaceSocket = io("/"); // Explicitly connects to the main namespace "/"
const orderSocket = io("/orders"); // Connects to the "/orders" namespace
const userSocket = io("/users"); // Connects to the "/users" namespace

// Cross-origin/Node.js version
const socketCrossOrigin = io("https://example.com"); // Connects to the main namespace "/" on example.com
const orderSocketCrossOrigin = io("https://example.com/orders"); // Connects to "/orders" on example.com
const userSocketCrossOrigin = io("https://example.com/users"); // Connects to "/users" on example.com
```

### Setting Up Multiple Namespaces and Event Handlers (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Demonstrates how to set up and handle connections for multiple namespaces ('/orders' and '/users') on the server side using Socket.IO. It shows how to define event listeners for specific events within each namespace.

```javascript
io.of("/orders").on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.on("order:list", () => {});
  socket.on("order:create", () => {});
});

io.of("/users").on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.on("user:list", () => {});
});
```

### Configure CORS with Cookies and Custom Headers for Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/handling-cors

This example demonstrates advanced CORS configuration for Socket.IO, enabling support for cookies (withCredentials) and custom request headers. It includes both server-side setup to allow these options and client-side code to send them. This is useful for applications requiring authenticated connections or custom data exchange.

```javascript
// server-side  
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {  
  cors: {  
    origin: "https://example.com",  
    methods: ["GET", "POST"],
    allowedHeaders: ["my-custom-header"],
    credentials: true  
  }
});  

// client-side  
const io = require("socket.io-client");  
const socket = io("https://api.example.com", {  
  withCredentials: true,  
  extraHeaders: {  
    "my-custom-header": "abcd"  
  }
});
```

### Implementing Middleware for Namespaces (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Shows how to implement middleware functions for Socket.IO namespaces to perform checks before a socket connection is fully established. This example demonstrates middleware for '/orders' and '/users' namespaces.

```javascript
const orderNamespace = io.of("/orders");

orderNamespace.use((socket, next) => {
  // ensure the socket has access to the "orders" namespace, and then
  next();
});

const userNamespace = io.of("/users");

userNamespace.use((socket, next) => {
  // ensure the socket has access to the "users" namespace, and then
  next();
});
```

### Configure Cookie Options in Socket.IO Server (v3.x)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Demonstrates how to configure cookie options for Socket.IO servers starting from version 3.x. The previous `cookieName`, `cookieHttpOnly`, and `cookiePath` options are now consolidated under a single `cookie` object. This change allows for more explicit control over cookie settings, such as name, HttpOnly flag, and path.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  cookie: {
    name: "test",
    httpOnly: false,
    path: "/custom"
  }
});
```

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-installation

```html
<script src="/socket.io/socket.io.js"></script>
<script>
  const socket = io();
</script>

### Join and Manage Rooms in Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

This example shows how a socket can join a named room and how to retrieve the list of rooms the socket is currently in. This is essential for broadcasting messages to specific groups of clients.

```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.join('room 237', () => {
    let rooms = Object.keys(socket.rooms);
    console.log(rooms); // [ <socket.id>, 'room 237' ]
  });
});
```

### Integrate Socket.IO with µWebSockets.js

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

Illustrates integrating Socket.IO with the high-performance µWebSockets.js library. This involves creating a µWebSockets.js App instance and then attaching the Socket.IO server to it using `io.attachApp()`. The example shows basic connection handling and listening on a specified port.

```javascript
import { App } from "uWebSockets.js";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const app = App();
const io = new Server();

io.attachApp(app);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

app.listen(3000, (token) => {
  if (!token) {
    console.warn("port already in use");
  }
});

```

### Integrate Socket.IO with Koa

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

Shows how to integrate Socket.IO with the Koa framework. Similar to Express, this involves creating an HTTP server using Koa's callback and attaching Socket.IO. The examples cover CommonJS, ES modules, and TypeScript syntax for initializing the Socket.IO server and handling connections.

```javascript
const Koa = require("koa");
const { createServer } = require("http");
const { Server } = require("socket.io");

const app = new Koa();
const httpServer = createServer(app.callback());
const io = new Server(httpServer, { /* options */ });

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);

```

```javascript
import Koa from "koa";
import { createServer } from "http";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const app = new Koa();
const httpServer = createServer(app.callback());
const io = new Server(httpServer, { /* options */ });

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);

```

```typescript

import { Server } from "socket.io";

});

### Engine.IO Protocol Handshake GET Request (WebSocket)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/engine-io-protocol

Illustrates the client's initial GET request to the server for establishing a connection using the WebSocket transport. This is part of the Engine.IO handshake process for WebSocket-only sessions.

```http
CLIENT                                                    SERVER  

  │                                                          │  
  │        GET /engine.io/?EIO=4&transport=websocket         │  
  │ ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────► │  
  │ ◄──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │  
  │                        HTTP 101                          │  
  │                                                          │  

```

### Create Basic Express Server (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-1

Sets up a minimal Express server using Node.js with CommonJS syntax. It creates an HTTP server, defines a root route that sends 'Hello world', and makes the server listen on port 3000.

```javascript
const express = require('express');
const { createServer } = require('node:http');

const app = express();
const server = createServer(app);

app.get('/', (req, res) => {
  res.send('<h1>Hello world</h1>');
});

server.listen(3000, () => {
  console.log('server running at http://localhost:3000');
});
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with GCP Pub/Sub Adapter

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/gcp-pubsub-adapter

Demonstrates how to initialize a Socket.IO server using the Google Cloud Pub/Sub adapter. It requires setting up a Pub/Sub client, topic, and then passing the adapter to the Socket.IO Server constructor. The adapter is then initialized before the server starts listening.

```javascript
import { PubSub } from "@google-cloud/pubsub";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/gcp-pubsub-adapter";  
  
const pubsub = new PubSub({  
  projectId: "your-project-id"  
});  
  
const topic = pubsub.topic(topicNameOrId);  
  
const io = new Server({  
  adapter: createAdapter(topic)  
});  
  
// wait for the creation of the pub/sub subscription  
await io.of("/").adapter.init();  
  
io.listen(3000);  

```

### Configure Socket.IO with Per-Message Deflate Enabled

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-initialization

This example shows how to enable and configure the 'perMessageDeflate' extension for WebSocket transport. It allows fine-tuning of compression parameters like chunk size, window bits, and context takeover settings.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  perMessageDeflate: {
    threshold: 2048,

    zlibDeflateOptions: {
      chunkSize: 8 * 1024,
    },

    zlibInflateOptions: {
      windowBits: 14,
      memLevel: 7,
    },

    clientNoContextTakeover: true,
    serverNoContextTakeover: true,
    serverMaxWindowBits: 10,

    concurrencyLimit: 20,
  }
});

```

### Integrate Socket.IO with express-session (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/faq

This example demonstrates how to integrate Socket.IO with express-session to manage user sessions for WebSocket connections. It requires the 'express', 'express-session', and 'socket.io' packages. The session middleware is applied to both the Express app and the Socket.IO connection handler.

```javascript
const express = require('express');
const session = require('express-session');
const app = express();

const server = require('http').createServer(app);
const io = require('socket.io')(server);

const sessionMiddleware = session({ secret: 'keyboard cat', cookie: { maxAge: 60000 }});
// register middleware in Express
app.use(sessionMiddleware);
// register middleware in Socket.IO
io.use((socket, next) => {
  sessionMiddleware(socket.request, {}, next);
  // sessionMiddleware(socket.request, socket.request.res, next); will not work with websocket-only
  // connections, as 'socket.request.res' will be undefined in that case
});

io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  const session = socket.request.session;
  session.connections++;
  session.save();
});

const port = process.env.PORT || 3000;
server.listen(port, () => console.log('server listening on port ' + port));

```

### Socket.IO Connection to Namespace Packet Example (Text)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Shows the text-based representation of a client initiating a connection to the main Socket.IO namespace ('/'). The server responds with a CONNECT packet including the session ID.

```text
CLIENT                                                      SERVER  
  
  │  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────►  │  
  │             { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/" }              │  
  │  ◄───────────────────────────────────────────────────────  │  
  │   { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/", data: { sid: "..." } }  │  
```

### Client Initialization

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Demonstrates how to initialize the Socket.IO client, either from the same domain or a different domain, and how to connect to custom namespaces.

```APIDOC
## Client Initialization

This section covers the initialization of the Socket.IO client library.

### Method
N/A (Client-side JavaScript)

### Endpoint
N/A (Client-side JavaScript)

### Description

Initialize the Socket.IO client. The `io` object is obtained either via a `<script>` tag or by importing the `socket.io-client` package.

#### From the same domain

If the front-end and back-end are served from the same domain, the client can be initialized without any arguments. The server URL is automatically deduced from the `window.location` object.

```javascript
const socket = io();
```

#### From a different domain

When the front-end and back-end are on different domains, provide the server's URL.

```javascript
const socket = io("https://server-domain.com");
```

**Note:** Ensure Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS) is enabled on the server. You can use `https://` or `wss://` (or `http://` and `ws://`). The following are equivalent:

```javascript
const socket = io("https://server-domain.com");
const socket = io("wss://server-domain.com");
const socket = io("server-domain.com"); // Browser only, served over https
```

#### Custom namespace

To connect to a specific namespace (instead of the default one), append the namespace path to the URL.

```javascript
// Same origin
const socket = io("/admin");

// Cross origin
const socket = io("https://server-domain.com/admin");
```

### Options

The `io()` function accepts an optional options object.

#### IO factory options

*   **`forceNew`** (boolean): Defaults to `false`. Creates a new Manager instance, disabling reuse of existing Managers.
    ```javascript
    // Creates 3 distinct WebSocket connections
    const socket = io("https://example.com", { forceNew: true });
    const productSocket = io("https://example.com/product", { forceNew: true });
    const orderSocket = io("https://example.com/order", { forceNew: true });
    ```
*   **`multiplex`** (boolean): Defaults to `true`. Reuses an existing Manager instance if available.

#### Low-level engine options

These options are shared across all Socket instances attached to the same Manager.

*   **`transports`** (string[]): Array of preferred transport methods (e.g., `['websocket', 'polling']`).
*   **`upgrade`** (boolean): Defaults to `true`. Whether to attempt upgrading the transport.
*   **`rememberUpgrade`** (boolean): Defaults to `true`. Whether to remember the upgraded transport for future connections.
*   **`path`** (string): The path for the Socket.IO server connection (default: `"/socket.io/"`).
*   **`query`** (object | string): Additional query parameters to send with the connection.
*   **`extraHeaders`** (object): Custom headers to send with the connection.
*   **`withCredentials`** (boolean): Whether to send cookies or authorization headers.
*   **`forceBase64`** (boolean): Forces base64 encoding for polling transport.
*   **`timestampRequests`** (boolean): Appends a timestamp to requests to prevent caching.
*   **`timestampParam`** (string): The name of the timestamp parameter.
*   **Node.js-specific options**: `agent`, `cert`, `rejectUnauthorized`.

#### Manager options

*   **`reconnection`** (boolean): Defaults to `true`. Whether to attempt reconnection.
*   **`reconnectionAttempts`** (number): Number of reconnection attempts.
*   **`reconnectionDelay`** (number): Initial delay between reconnection attempts.
*   **`reconnectionDelayMax`** (number): Maximum delay between reconnection attempts.
*   **`randomizationFactor`** (number): Factor for randomizing reconnection delay.
*   **`timeout`** (number): Connection timeout in milliseconds.
*   **`autoConnect`** (boolean): Defaults to `true`. Whether to automatically connect.
*   **`parser`** (object): Custom parser for encoding/decoding messages.

#### Socket options

*   **`auth`** (object): Object containing authentication credentials to be sent during connection.

### Request Example

```javascript
// Example of connecting with options
const socket = io("https://example.com", {
  reconnection: true,
  reconnectionAttempts: 5,
  auth: {
    token: "123"
  }
});
```

### Response

N/A (Client-side initialization does not typically involve a direct response in this context; successful connection is indicated by event listeners.)

```

### Client Initialization with io()

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-api

Demonstrates how to initialize a Socket.IO client connection using the `io()` function in both browser and Node.js environments.

```APIDOC
## Client API - io()

### Description
Initializes a Socket.IO client connection. It can be used in a browser environment by including the Socket.IO script, or in a Node.js environment by requiring the `socket.io-client` package.

### Method
`io([url][, options])`

### Endpoint
N/A (Client-side function)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
N/A

#### Query Parameters
N/A

#### Request Body
N/A

### Usage Examples
#### Browser
```html
<script src="/socket.io/socket.io.js"></script>
<script>
  const socket = io('http://localhost');
</script>
```

#### Node.js
```javascript
const io = require('socket.io-client');
// or with import syntax
// import io from 'socket.io-client';

const socket = io('http://localhost');
```

### `io.protocol`
* _(Number)_ The protocol revision number (currently: 4).

### Initialization with Options
* `url` _(String)_ (defaults to `window.location.host`)
* `options` _(Object)_
  * `forceNew` _(Boolean)_ whether to reuse an existing connection (defaults to `false`)

**Returns**: `Socket`

Creates a new `Manager` for the given URL and returns a `Socket` instance for a namespace. Query parameters can be provided via the `query` option or directly in the URL.

### Example with Options
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io-client");

const socket = io("ws://example.com/my-namespace", {
  reconnectionDelayMax: 10000,
  query: {
    auth: "123"
  }
});
```

This is equivalent to:

```javascript
const { Manager } = require("socket.io-client");

const manager = new Manager("ws://example.com", {
  reconnectionDelayMax: 10000
});

const socket = manager.socket("/my-namespace", {
  query: {
    auth: "123"
  }
});
```

### Initialization Examples
#### With multiplexing (default behavior)
```javascript
const socket = io();
const adminSocket = io('/admin');
// A single connection is established for both.
```

#### With `forceNew: true` (disables multiplexing)
```javascript
const socket = io();
const adminSocket = io('/admin', { forceNew: true });
// Two distinct connections are created.

const socket2 = io();
// This also creates two distinct connections.
```

#### With custom `path`
```javascript
// Client-side
const socket = io('http://localhost', {
  path: '/myownpath'
});

// Server-side
const io = require('socket.io')({
  path: '/myownpath'
});
```

Request URLs will look like: `localhost/myownpath/?EIO=3&transport=polling&sid=<id>`

#### With query parameters in URL
```javascript
const socket = io('http://localhost?token=abc');

// Server-side middleware example
const io = require('socket.io')();
io.use((socket, next) => {
  let token = socket.handshake.query.token;
  if (isValid(token)) {
    return next();
  } 
  return next(new Error('authentication error'));
});
```

#### With `query` option
```javascript
const socket = io({
  query: {
    token: 'cde'
  }
});

// Updating query on reconnection
socket.on('reconnect_attempt', () => {
  socket.io.opts.query = {
    token: 'fgh'
  };
});
```
```

### Catch-all Listeners

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Introduces 'catch-all' listeners (`onAny`, `prependAny`, `offAny`, `listenersAny`) for both server and client, allowing handling of any event.

```APIDOC
## Catch-all Event Listeners

### Description
Provides methods for registering, removing, and retrieving listeners that catch all emitted events on a socket.

### Method
`onAny`, `prependAny`, `offAny`, `listenersAny`

### Endpoint
N/A

### Parameters
#### `onAny` / `prependAny` Parameters
- **listener** (function) - The function to be called when any event is received. It accepts the event name and its arguments.

#### `offAny` Parameters
- **listener** (function) - Optional - If provided, removes only this specific listener. If omitted, all `onAny` listeners are removed.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Server-side
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // Listen for any event
  socket.onAny((event, ...args) => {
    console.log(`Received event: ${event} with args: ${args}`);
  });

  // Listen for any event, but prepend this listener
  socket.prependAny((event, ...args) => {});

  // Remove all 'onAny' listeners
  socket.offAny();

  // Remove a specific 'onAny' listener
  const myListener = (event, ...args) => {};
  socket.onAny(myListener);
  socket.offAny(myListener);

  // Get all 'onAny' listeners
  const listeners = socket.listenersAny();
});

// Client-side
const socket = io();
socket.onAny((event, ...args) => {});
socket.prependAny((event, ...args) => {});
socket.offAny(); // remove all listeners
socket.offAny(listener);
const listeners = socket.listenersAny();
```
```

### Server Initialization

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Initializes a new Socket.IO server instance with optional configuration. The server can be attached to an existing HTTP server or start listening on a specified port.

```APIDOC
## Server Initialization and Attachment

### Description
Initializes a new Socket.IO server instance and attaches it to an HTTP server or a specified port.

### Method
`new Server(options)`

### Parameters
#### Options (Object)
- **path** (String) - The path under which engine.io and static files will be served. Defaults to `/socket.io`.
- **serveClient** (Boolean) - Whether to serve client files. Defaults to `true`.
- **pingInterval** (Number) - Milliseconds between pings. Defaults to `10000`.
- **pingTimeout** (Number) - Milliseconds without response before considering a client disconnected. Defaults to `5000`.
- **cookie** (Boolean|Object) - Configuration for the session cookie. Defaults to `true`.

### Request Example
```javascript
const io = require('socket.io')({ 
  path: '/test',
  serveClient: false 
});

// Attaching to an existing HTTP server
const server = require('http').createServer();
io.attach(server, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
server.listen(3000);

// Attaching to a port
io.attach(3000, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```
```

--------------------------------

### Setup Socket.IO with Hono and Bun

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

This snippet shows how to configure Socket.IO with Hono specifically for the Bun runtime. It utilizes '@socket.io/bun-engine' for efficient handling within Bun. The code sets up both the Socket.IO server and the Hono application, binding them together and defining the request handling logic for both WebSocket and standard HTTP requests.

```javascript
import { Server as Engine } from "@socket.io/bun-engine";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { Hono } from "hono";  
  
const io = new Server();  
  
const engine = new Engine();  
  
io.bind(engine);  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  // ...  
});  
  
const app = new Hono();  
  
const { websocket } = engine.handler();  
  
export default {
    port: 3000,  
    idleTimeout: 30, // must be greater than the "pingInterval" option of the engine, which defaults to 25 seconds  
  
    fetch(req, server) {
      const url = new URL(req.url);  
  
      if (url.pathname === "/socket.io/") {
        return engine.handleRequest(req, server);  
      } else {
        return app.fetch(req, server);  
      }
    },
  
    websocket
}


```

--------------------------------

### Basic Socket.IO Server Setup (ES Modules)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-3

Sets up a basic Socket.IO server using Express and the Node.js HTTP server with ES module syntax. It listens for incoming connections and logs a message when a user connects. Dependencies include express and socket.io.

```javascript
import express from 'express';  
import { createServer } from 'node:http';  
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';  
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';  
import { Server } from 'socket.io';  

const app = express();  
const server = createServer(app);  
const io = new Server(server);  

const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));  

app.get('/', (req, res) => {  
  res.sendFile(join(__dirname, 'index.html'));  
});  

io.on('connection', (socket) => {  
  console.log('a user connected');  
});  

server.listen(3000, () => {  
  console.log('server running at http://localhost:3000');  
});  

```

### Initialize Socket.IO Manager with Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-api

Demonstrates how to initialize a Socket.IO Manager with custom options. This includes settings for the connection path, reconnection behavior, timeouts, and query parameters. The Manager is the entry point for creating and managing socket connections.

```javascript
import { Manager } from "socket.io-client";

const manager = new Manager("https://example.com", {
  path: "/my-socket-io-path",
  reconnection: true,
  reconnectionAttempts: 5,
  reconnectionDelay: 2000,
  reconnectionDelayMax: 10000,
  randomizationFactor: 0.3,
  timeout: 15000,
  autoConnect: false,
  query: {
    token: "your_auth_token"
  }
});

// To open the connection manually when autoConnect is false:
// manager.open();
```

### Setup Socket.IO with Hono in Node.js (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

This snippet demonstrates how to integrate Socket.IO with Hono using CommonJS modules in a Node.js environment. It requires the 'hono' and '@hono/node-server' packages for the web server and 'socket.io' for real-time communication. The code sets up an HTTP server, attaches Hono's fetch handler, and then initializes Socket.IO.

```javascript
const { Hono } = require("hono");  
const { serve } = require("@hono/node-server");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
  
const app = new Hono();  
  
const httpServer = serve({
    fetch: app.fetch,  
    port: 3000,  
});  
  
const io = new Server(httpServer, {  
    /* options */  
});  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
    // ...  
});  


```

### Configure Socket.IO with Bun Engine

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-installation

Sets up a Socket.IO server to use the `@socket.io/bun-engine` for enhanced performance within the Bun runtime. This configuration involves binding the Socket.IO server instance to the Bun engine handler.

```javascript
import { Server as Engine } from "@socket.io/bun-engine";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server();

const engine = new Engine({
  path: "/socket.io/",
});

io.bind(engine);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

export default {
  port: 3000,
  idleTimeout: 30, // must be greater than the "pingInterval" option of the engine, which defaults to 25 seconds

  ...engine.handler(),
};
```

### Dynamic Namespace Creation for Tenants (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/namespaces

Illustrates using a regular expression to dynamically create namespaces based on connection paths, suitable for multi-tenant applications where each tenant gets its own communication channel.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io");

// Matches any path that starts with / followed by one or more word characters (e.g., /tenant1, /workspaceA)
const workspaces = io.of(/^\/\w+$/);

workspaces.on("connection", (socket) => {
  const workspace = socket.nsp; // 'socket.nsp' refers to the namespace the socket connected to
  console.log(`Client connected to workspace namespace: ${workspace.name}`);

  // Emit a message to all clients within this specific workspace namespace
  workspace.emit("hello", `Welcome to workspace ${workspace.name}`);

  socket.on("disconnect", () => {
    console.log(`Client disconnected from workspace namespace: ${workspace.name}`);
  });
});
```

--------------------------------

### Server Attachment and Listening

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Attaches the Socket.IO server to an HTTP server or starts listening on a port.

```APIDOC
## Server Attachment and Listening

### Description
Attaches the Socket.IO server to an existing HTTP server or starts listening on a specified port.

### Methods
`server.attach(httpServer[, options])`
  - `httpServer` (http.Server) - The server to attach to.
  - `options` (Object) - Optional configuration for attachment.

`server.attach(port[, options])`
  - `port` (Number) - The port to listen on.
  - `options` (Object) - Optional configuration for attachment.

`server.listen(httpServer[, options])` - Synonym for `server.attach(httpServer[, options])`.
`server.listen(port[, options])` - Synonym for `server.attach(port[, options])`.
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with Options and Attach to HTTP Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Shows how to initialize a Socket.IO server using only an options object and then attach it to an existing http.Server instance or directly to a port. This provides flexibility in managing the underlying HTTP server.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")({
  path: "/test",
  serveClient: false,
});

// either
const server = require("http").createServer();

io.attach(server, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});

server.listen(3000);

// or
io.attach(3000, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```

--------------------------------

### Server Initialization

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Demonstrates different ways to initialize the Socket.IO Server, either by binding to an existing HTTP server, listening on a port, or with options only.

```APIDOC
## Server Initialization

### `new Server(httpServer[, options])`

Binds the Socket.IO server to an existing Node.js HTTP server.

- `httpServer` (http.Server): The server to bind to.
- `options` (Object): Optional configuration for the Socket.IO server and its underlying Engine.IO server.

**Example Usage:**
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")();
// or
const { Server } = require("socket.io");
const io = new Server();
```

### `new Server(port[, options])`

Creates a new HTTP server and listens on the specified port.

- `port` (Number): The port number to listen on.
- `options` (Object): Optional configuration for the Socket.IO server and its underlying Engine.IO server.

**Example Usage:**
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(3000, {
  path: "/test",
  serveClient: false,
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```

### `new Server(options)`

Initializes the Socket.IO server with provided options without immediately attaching to a server or port.

- `options` (Object): Optional configuration for the Socket.IO server and its underlying Engine.IO server.

**Example Usage:**
```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")({
  path: "/test",
  serveClient: false,
});

// Attach to an existing HTTP server
const server = require("http").createServer();
io.attach(server, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
server.listen(3000);

// Or attach directly to a port
io.attach(3000, {
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```

### Available Options

Several options can be passed during initialization. These include core Socket.IO options and options for the underlying Engine.IO server.

**Socket.IO Options:**

| Option           | Default Value | Description                                                                                         |
|------------------|---------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `path`           | `/socket.io`  | Name of the path to capture                                                                         |
| `serveClient`    | `true`        | Whether to serve the client files                                                                   |
| `adapter`        | `Adapter`     | The adapter to use. Defaults to a memory-based adapter. See [socket.io-adapter](link_to_adapter_docs). |
| `parser`         | `Parser`      | The parser to use. Defaults to the built-in parser. See [socket.io-parser](link_to_parser_docs).     |
| `connectTimeout` | `45000`       | Milliseconds before closing a client that has not joined a namespace.                               |

**Engine.IO Options:**

| Option             | Default Value             | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|--------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `pingTimeout`      | `5000`                    | Milliseconds without a pong packet to consider the connection closed.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `pingInterval`     | `25000`                   | Milliseconds before sending a new ping packet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `upgradeTimeout`   | `10000`                   | Milliseconds before an uncompleted transport upgrade is cancelled.                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `maxHttpBufferSize`| `1e6`                     | Maximum bytes or characters a message can be before closing the session (to avoid DoS).                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `allowRequest`     | `null`                    | A function to decide whether to continue a handshake or upgrade request. `fn(err, success)` must be called.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `transports`       | `["polling", "websocket"]` | Allowed connection transports.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `allowUpgrades`    | `true`                    | Whether to allow transport upgrades.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `perMessageDeflate`| `false`                   | Parameters for WebSocket permessage-deflate extension. Set to `true` to enable.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `httpCompression`  | `true`                    | Parameters for HTTP compression for polling transports. Set to `false` to disable.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `wsEngine`         | `ws`                      | WebSocket server implementation to use. Must conform to the `ws` interface. Can also be `eiows`.                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `cors`             | `null`                    | Options to be forwarded to the [cors](https://www.npmjs.com/package/cors) module.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `cookie`           | `null`                    | Options to be forwarded to the [cookie](https://www.npmjs.com/package/cookie) module.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `allowEIO3`        | `false`                   | Whether to enable compatibility with Socket.IO v2 clients.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with TypeScript

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-initialization

Initializes a Socket.IO server using TypeScript, including type definitions for better code safety and autocompletion. Requires installing `@types/socket.io`.

```typescript
import { createServer } from "http";
import Server = require("socket.io");
import { Socket } from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer();
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  // ...
});

io.on("connection", (socket: Socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);

```

### Configure Basic Authentication for Socket.IO Admin UI

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/admin-ui

This example configures basic authentication for the Socket.IO admin UI. It uses a username and a bcrypt-hashed password. Ensure the password hash is generated correctly.

```javascript
instrument(io, {  
  auth: {  
    type: "basic",  
    username: "admin",  
    password: "$2b$10$heqvAkYMez.Va6Et2uXInOnkCT6/uQj1brkrbyG3LpopDklcq7ZOS" // "changeit" encrypted with bcrypt  
  },  
});  
```

### Broadcast Chat Message to All (Server-Side)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-5

This example broadcasts a received chat message to all connected clients, including the sender, using `io.emit()`. It captures a 'chat message' event from a client and then re-emits it to all connected sockets. This is a fundamental pattern for real-time chat applications.

```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.on('chat message', (msg) => {
    io.emit('chat message', msg);
  });
});
```

### Dynamically Creating Namespaces for Tenants (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Shows how to dynamically create Socket.IO namespaces using a regular expression to accommodate multiple tenants. Each tenant gets a unique namespace, and connections within these namespaces can emit events.

```javascript
const workspaces = io.of(/^\/\w+$/);

workspaces.on("connection", socket => {
  const workspace = socket.nsp;

  workspace.emit("hello");
});
```

### Socket.IO Custom Namespace Connection Packet Example (Text)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Demonstrates the text-based packet exchange for connecting to a custom Socket.IO namespace ('/admin'). This includes the client sending a CONNECT packet and the server responding with the session ID.

```text
Client > { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/admin" }  
Server > { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/admin", data: { sid: "oSO0OpakMV_3jnilAAAA" } }  
```

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-options

This example shows how to enable compatibility with Socket.IO v2 clients by setting the `allowEIO3` option to `true`. By default, this is set to `false` for compatibility with newer clients.

```javascript
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  allowEIO3: true // false by default
});
```

### Get Connected Clients Count - Node.js

### Join Rooms with Socket.IO SocketsJoin

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Shows how to make Socket instances join specified rooms. This can be applied to all sockets, sockets within a specific room, or sockets within a specific namespace.

```javascript
// make all Socket instances join the "room1" room
io.socketsJoin("room1");

// make all Socket instances in the "room1" room join the "room2" and "room3" rooms
io.in("room1").socketsJoin(["room2", "room3"]);

// make all Socket instances in the "room1" room of the "admin" namespace join the "room2" room
io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsJoin("room2");

// this also works with a single socket ID
io.in(theSocketId).socketsJoin("room1");
```

### socket.use(fn)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Registers a middleware, which is a function that gets executed for every incoming Packet.

```APIDOC
## `socket.use(fn)`

### Description
Registers a middleware, which is a function that gets executed for every incoming `Packet` and receives as parameter the packet and a function to optionally defer execution to the next registered middleware. Errors passed to middleware callbacks are sent as special `error` packets to clients.

### Parameters
* `fn` _(Function)_ - The middleware function.

### Usage
```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.use((packet, next) => {
    if (packet.doge === true) return next();
    next(new Error('Not a doge error'));
  });
});
```
```

--------------------------------

### Implement Socket.IO Middleware

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

This example illustrates how to register and use middleware functions in Socket.IO. Middleware can intercept incoming packets and perform actions like validation before they are processed by the application.

```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.use((packet, next) => {
    if (packet.doge === true) return next();
    next(new Error('Not a doge error'));
  });
});
```

### Socket.IO Test Suite with Mocha and Chai (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

A comprehensive test suite for a Socket.IO project using Mocha for test execution and Chai for assertions. It demonstrates setting up an HTTP server, establishing client connections, and testing basic event emission, acknowledgements, and custom asynchronous helpers.

```javascript
const { createServer } = require("node:http");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
const ioc = require("socket.io-client");  
const { assert } = require("chai");  
  
function waitFor(socket, event) {  
  return new Promise((resolve) => {  
    socket.once(event, resolve);  
  });  
}  
  
describe("my awesome project", () => {  
  let io, serverSocket, clientSocket;  
  
  before((done) => {  
    const httpServer = createServer();  
    io = new Server(httpServer);  
    httpServer.listen(() => {  
      const port = httpServer.address().port;  
      clientSocket = ioc(`http://localhost:${port}`);  
      io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
        serverSocket = socket;  
      });  
      clientSocket.on("connect", done);  
    });  
  });  
  
  after(() => {  
    io.close();  
    clientSocket.disconnect();  
  });  
  
  it("should work", (done) => {  
    clientSocket.on("hello", (arg) => {  
      assert.equal(arg, "world");  
      done();  
    });  
    serverSocket.emit("hello", "world");  
  });  
  
  it("should work with an acknowledgement", (done) => {  
    serverSocket.on("hi", (cb) => {  
      cb("hola");  
    });  
    clientSocket.emit("hi", (arg) => {  
      assert.equal(arg, "hola");  
      done();  
    });  
  });  
  
  it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {  
    serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {  
      cb("bar");  
    });  
    const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");  
    assert.equal(result, "bar");  
  });  
  
  it("should work with waitFor()", () => {  
    clientSocket.emit("baz");  
  
    return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");  
  });  
});  

```
