### Server Instantiation with Port

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Demonstrates how to create a Socket.IO server instance that listens on a specified port, creating an HTTP server internally.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io/port

### Description
Instantiates a new Socket.IO server and starts listening on a specified port. A new HTTP server is created internally if one is not provided.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/websites/socket_io/port

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **port** (Number) - Required - The port number for the Socket.IO server to listen on.
- **options** (Object) - Optional - Configuration options for the Socket.IO server. See the options for `new Server(httpServer, options)` above.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "port": 3000,
  "options": {
    "path": "/test",
    "serveClient": false,
    "pingInterval": 10000,
    "pingTimeout": 5000,
    "cookie": false
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **io** (Object) - The instantiated Socket.IO server object.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "io": "<Socket.IO Server Instance>"
}
```
```

### Socket.IO Client-Side Communication

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2

Demonstrates how to establish a Socket.IO connection from the client-side using the Socket.IO JavaScript library. It shows how to send messages using both 'send()' and custom 'emit()' events, and how to handle incoming 'message' and custom events.

```javascript
const socket = io('ws://localhost:3000');

socket.on('connect', () => {
  // either with send()
  socket.send('Hello!');

  // or with emit() and custom event names
  socket.emit('salutations', 'Hello!', { 'mr': 'john' }, Uint8Array.from([1, 2, 3, 4]));
});

// handle the event sent with socket.send()
socket.on('message', data => {
  console.log(data);
});

// handle the event sent with socket.emit()
socket.on('greetings', (elem1, elem2, elem3) => {
  console.log(elem1, elem2, elem3);
});
```

### Enable Trying All Socket.IO Transports (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

This example shows how to configure the Socket.IO client to attempt all available network transports if the initial one fails. This is useful when the server might have specific transport configurations or when prioritizing WebSocket can lead to connection delays.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client"; 

const socket = io({
  tryAllTransports: true
});
```

### Namespace.allSockets()

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The `Namespace.clients()` function has been renamed to `Namespace.allSockets()` and now returns a Promise. It retrieves a list of socket IDs connected to a specific namespace.

```APIDOC
## GET /namespaces/{namespace}/sockets

### Description
Retrieves a list of socket IDs connected to a specific namespace.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
`/namespaces/{namespace}/sockets`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **namespace** (string) - Required - The namespace to retrieve socket IDs from. Use "" for the default namespace.

#### Query Parameters
None

### Request Example
```
// For the default namespace
const ids = await io.allSockets();

// For the "chat" namespace
const ids = await io.of("/chat").allSockets();

// For the "chat" namespace and the "general" room
const ids = await io.of("/chat").in("general").allSockets();
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **ids** (array) - An array of socket IDs.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "ids": ["6em3d4TJP8Et9EMNAAAA", "G5p55dHhGgUnLUctAAAB"]
}
```
```

### Usage with Node.js Cluster

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/cluster-adapter

Demonstrates how to set up Socket.IO with the cluster adapter in a Node.js cluster environment. It includes configurations for both the master and worker processes, enabling sticky sessions and inter-worker communication.

```javascript
const cluster = require("cluster");  
const http = require("http");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
const numCPUs = require("os").cpus().length;  
const { setupMaster, setupWorker } = require("@socket.io/sticky");  
const { createAdapter, setupPrimary } = require("@socket.io/cluster-adapter");  
  
if (cluster.isMaster) {  
  console.log(`Master ${process.pid} is running`);  
  
  const httpServer = http.createServer();  
  
  // setup sticky sessions  
  setupMaster(httpServer, {  
    loadBalancingMethod: "least-connection",  
  });  
  
  // setup connections between the workers  
  setupPrimary();  
  
  // needed for packets containing buffers (you can ignore it if you only send plaintext objects)  
  // Node.js < 16.0.0  
  cluster.setupMaster({  
    serialization: "advanced",  
  });  
  // Node.js > 16.0.0  
  // cluster.setupPrimary({  
  //   serialization: "advanced",  
  // });  
  
  httpServer.listen(3000);  
  
  for (let i = 0; i < numCPUs; i++) {  
    cluster.fork();  
  }  
  
  cluster.on("exit", (worker) => {  
    console.log(`Worker ${worker.process.pid} died`);  
    cluster.fork();  
  });  
} else {  
  console.log(`Worker ${process.pid} started`);  
  
  const httpServer = http.createServer();  
  const io = new Server(httpServer);  
  
  // use the cluster adapter  
  io.adapter(createAdapter());  
  
  // setup connection with the primary process  
  setupWorker(io);  
  
  io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
    /* ... */  
  });  
}  

```

### Socket.IO Client Bundles for Development and Production

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Shows how to include Socket.IO client-side bundles. The `socket.io.js` is unminified with debug information for development, while `socket.io.min.js` is the minified production version. An additional `socket.io.msgpack.min.js` is available for production with msgpack parsing.

```html
<!-- during development -->  
<script src="/socket.io/socket.io.js"></script>  
<!-- for production -->  
<script src="/socket.io/socket.io.min.js"></script>  
```

### namespace.allSockets()

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Gets a list of socket IDs connected to this namespace, potentially across multiple nodes if using an adapter.

```APIDOC
## GET /namespace.allSockets

### Description
Gets a list of socket IDs connected to this namespace (across all nodes if applicable).

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/namespace.allSockets

### Parameters
No parameters.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **Set<String>** - A Set of socket IDs.

### Request Example
```javascript
// all sockets in the main namespace  
const ids = await io.allSockets();  
  
// all sockets in the main namespace and in the "user:1234" room  
const ids = await io.in("user:1234").allSockets();  
  
// all sockets in the "chat" namespace  
const ids = await io.of("/chat").allSockets();  
  
// all sockets in the "chat" namespace and in the "general" room  
const ids = await io.of("/chat").in("general").allSockets();  
```
```

### Socket.IO Connection Packet Encoding

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Provides examples of encoded packets for establishing a connection to a Socket.IO namespace, including the main namespace, custom namespaces, and connection errors.

```json
{ type: CONNECT, namespace: "/" }
```

```text
0
```

```json
{ type: CONNECT, namespace: "/admin", data: { sid: "oSO0OpakMV_3jnilAAAA" } }
```

```text
0/admin,{"sid":"oSO0OpakMV_3jnilAAAA"}
```

```json
{ type: CONNECT_ERROR, namespace: "/", data: { message: "Not authorized" } }

4{"message":"Not authorized"}

### Handle Disconnect Due to Ping Timeout

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-initialization

This example shows how to listen for the 'disconnect' event and log the reason, specifically when a disconnection occurs due to a ping timeout. This is part of the heartbeat mechanism to ensure connection liveness.

```javascript
socket.on("disconnect", (reason) => {
  console.log(reason); // "ping timeout"
});

```

--------------------------------

### Broadcast to User's Devices - JavaScript

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/rooms

Provides an example of broadcasting data to all devices or tabs belonging to a specific user by joining them to a room identified by their `userId`.

```javascript
io.on("connection", async (socket) => {
  const userId = await computeUserIdFromHeaders(socket);

  socket.join(userId);

  // and then later
  io.to(userId).emit("hi");
});
```

### Socket.IO Connection Refused Packet Example (Text)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Shows the text-based packet exchange when a Socket.IO connection to a namespace is refused by the server. The server responds with a CONNECT_ERROR packet, indicating the reason for refusal.

```text
Client > { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/" }  
Server > { type: CONNECT_ERROR, namespace: "/", data: { message: "Not authorized" } }  
```

### server.emit()

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Emits an event to all connected clients in the main namespace. Any number of serializable arguments can be included. Supports acknowledgements starting from v4.5.0.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io/server.emit

### Description
Emits an event to all connected clients in the main namespace. Arguments will be automatically serialized.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/websites/socket_io/server.emit

#### Query Parameters
- **eventName** (string | symbol) - The name of the event to emit.
- **args** (any[]) - An array of arguments to send with the event.
- **timeout** (number) - Optional - The timeout in milliseconds for acknowledgements (required if using acknowledgements).

### Request Example
```json
{
  "eventName": "chat message",
  "args": ["John Doe", "Hello there!"],
  "timeout": 10000
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **status** (string) - Indicates the status of the emit operation.
- **responses** (any[]) - An array of responses from clients if acknowledgements were used.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "status": "Emitted successfully",
  "responses": ["ack1", "ack2"]
}
```
```

```typescript
it("should work with emitWithAck()", async () => {
  serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
    cb("bar");
  });
  const result = await clientSocket.emitWithAck("foo");
  expect(result).toEqual("bar");
});

### autoUnref Option

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

Configures the Socket.IO client to allow program exit when no other active timers or sockets are present.

```APIDOC
## autoUnref Option

### Description
The `autoUnref` option, when set to `true`, allows the Node.js process to exit if the Socket.IO client is the only remaining active timer or socket.

### Method
`io()` constructor

### Endpoint
N/A (Client-side option)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **autoUnref** (boolean) - Optional - If set to `true`, the client will not prevent the Node.js process from exiting. Defaults to `false`.

### Request Example
```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  autoUnref: true
});
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
None (This is a client-side configuration option)

#### Response Example
None

### Note
This option only applies to Node.js clients.
```

### Manually Connect and Disconnect Socket (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-api

Provides examples for manually initiating a connection using `socket.connect()` and closing the connection with `socket.disconnect()`. `socket.connect()` can also be used for manual reconnection after a disconnection.

```javascript
const socket = io({
  autoConnect: false
});

// ...
socket.connect();

```

```javascript
socket.on("disconnect", () => {
  socket.connect();
});

```

```javascript
socket.disconnect();

```

### Migration from socket.io-redis

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Instructions for migrating from the older `socket.io-redis` package to `@socket.io/redis-adapter`.

```APIDOC
## Migrating from `socket.io-redis`

### Description
Instructions for migrating from the older `socket.io-redis` package to `@socket.io/redis-adapter`.

### Before
```javascript
const redisAdapter = require("socket.io-redis");

io.adapter(redisAdapter({ host: "localhost", port: 6379 }));
```

### After
```javascript
const { createClient } = require("redis");
const { createAdapter } = require("@socket.io/redis-adapter");

const pubClient = createClient({ url: "redis://localhost:6379" });
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();

io.adapter(createAdapter(pubClient, subClient));
```

### Tip
The communication protocol between Socket.IO servers has not changed, allowing for mixed versions of the adapter.
```

--------------------------------

### Typed Events

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

Enables type safety for events exchanged between client and server using TypeScript interfaces.

```APIDOC
## Typed Events

### Description
Allows defining specific event signatures using TypeScript interfaces for improved type checking and code completion in both client and server environments.

### Client-Side Usage

#### Interface Definition
Define interfaces for events sent from client to server and vice-versa.
```typescript
interface ClientToServerEvents {
  noArg: () => void;
  basicEmit: (a: number, b: string, c: number[]) => void;
}

interface ServerToClientEvents {
  withAck: (d: string, cb: (e: number) => void) => void;
}
```

#### Client Initialization
Initialize the Socket.IO client with the defined event types.
```typescript
import { io, Socket } from "socket.io-client";

const socket: Socket<ServerToClientEvents, ClientToServerEvents> = io();

socket.emit("noArg");
socket.emit("basicEmit", 1, "2", [3]);

socket.on("withAck", (d, cb) => {
  cb(4);
});
```

### Server-Side Usage

#### Interface Definition (Reversed)
On the server, the `ServerToClientEvents` and `ClientToServerEvents` interfaces are reversed compared to the client.
```typescript
interface ClientToServerEvents {
  noArg: () => void;
  basicEmit: (a: number, b: string, c: number[]) => void;
}

interface ServerToClientEvents {
  withAck: (d: string, cb: (e: number) => void) => void;
}
```

#### Server Initialization
Initialize the Socket.IO server with the defined event types.
```typescript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server<ClientToServerEvents, ServerToClientEvents>(3000);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.on("noArg", () => {
    // ...
  });

  socket.on("basicEmit", (a, b, c) => {
    // ...
  });

  socket.emit("withAck", "42", (e) => {
    console.log(e);
  });
});
```

### Default Behavior
By default, events are untyped, and arguments are inferred as `any`.
```

### Socket.IO Default Parser Encoding Examples

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/custom-parser

Illustrates how the default Socket.IO parser encodes different types of events, including basic events and events with binary data, acknowledgements, and custom namespaces.

```javascript
socket.emit("test", 42);
```

```text
2["test",42]
||
|└─ JSON-encoded payload
└─ packet type (2 => EVENT)
```

```javascript
socket.emit("test", Uint8Array.from([42]), () => {
  console.log("ack received");
});
```

```text
51-/admin,13["test",{"_placeholder":true,"num":0}]
||||     || └─ JSON-encoded payload with placeholders for binary attachments
||||     |└─ acknowledgement id
||||     └─ separator
|||└─ namespace (not included when it's the main namespace)
||└─ separator
|└─ number of binary attachments
└─ packet type (5 => BINARY EVENT)

and an additional attachment (the extracted Uint8Array)
```

  // options go here

--------------------------------

### Nginx Configuration for Sticky Load Balancing (Nginx Configuration)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/using-multiple-nodes

An example Nginx configuration file demonstrating how to set up sticky load balancing for Socket.IO applications. It uses the `hash` directive based on client IP address to ensure requests from the same client are routed to the same server instance.

```nginx
http {  
  server {  
    listen 3000;  
    server_name io.yourhost.com;  
  
    location / {  
      proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;  
      proxy_set_header Host $host;  
  
      proxy_pass http://nodes;  
  
      # enable WebSockets  
      proxy_http_version 1.1;  
      proxy_set_header Upgrade $http_upgrade;  
      proxy_set_header Connection "upgrade";  
    }  
  }  
  
  upstream nodes {  
    # enable sticky session with either "hash" (uses the complete IP address)  
    hash $remote_addr consistent;  
    # or "ip_hash" (uses the first three octets of the client IPv4 address, or the entire IPv6 address)  
    # ip_hash;  
    # or "sticky" (needs commercial subscription)  
    # sticky cookie srv_id expires=1h domain=.example.com path=/;  
  
    server app01:3000;  
    server app02:3000;  
    server app03:3000;  
  }  
}  
```

### Dynamic Namespace Priority Example

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/namespaces

Illustrates the priority of existing namespaces over dynamic ones. If a specific namespace like '/dynamic-101' is registered explicitly, connections to it will not trigger the dynamic namespace handler for the regex pattern.

```javascript
// register "dynamic-101" namespace
io.of("/dynamic-101");

io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/).on("connection", (socket) => {
  // will not be called for a connection on the "dynamic-101" namespace
});
```

### Handle Connection Errors with Socket.IO Engine

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Provides an example of how to handle abnormal connection closures by listening to the `connection_error` event on the Engine.IO server. It logs details about the request, error code, message, and context.

```javascript
io.engine.on("connection_error", (err) => {
  console.log(err.req);
  console.log(err.code);
  console.log(err.message);
  console.log(err.context);
});
```

### server.path([value])

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Sets or gets the path under which engine.io and static files are served. Defaults to '/socket.io/'. The client-side path must match the server-side path.

```APIDOC
## GET/PUT /websites/socket_io/path

### Description
Sets or retrieves the path under which `engine.io` and static files will be served. The default path is `/socket.io/`. It is crucial that the client-side path configuration matches this server-side path.

### Method
GET (to retrieve current path), PUT (to set new path)

### Endpoint
`/websites/socket_io/path`

### Parameters
#### Request Body (for PUT)
- **value** (string) - Optional - The new path to serve Socket.IO under. If omitted, the current path is returned.

### Request Example
```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server();

// Set a custom path
io.path("/myownpath/");

// Retrieve the current path
const currentPath = io.path(); 
console.log(currentPath); // Output: "/myownpath/"

// Client-side configuration must match:
import { io } from "socket.io-client";
const socket = io({ path: "/myownpath/" });
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **value** (string) - The current path configuration for the server.
```

```javascript
io.sockets.emit('eventname', 'eventdata');

```javascript
io.emit('eventname', 'eventdata');

const socket1 = io(); // 1st manager  
const socket2 = io(); // 2nd manager  
const socket3 = io("/admin"); // reuse the 1st manager  
const socket4 = io("/admin"); // 3rd manager

### Joining Rooms with `socketsJoin`

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

The `socketsJoin` utility method allows all matching socket instances to join specified rooms. It can be applied globally or filtered by namespace and existing rooms.

```javascript
// make all Socket instances join the "room1" room
io.socketsJoin("room1");

// make all Socket instances of the "admin" namespace in the "room1" room join the "room2" room
io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsJoin("room2");
```

### Broadcasting to Multiple Rooms

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

## POST /io.to()

### Description
Broadcasts a message to clients in multiple specified rooms.

`io.to()` or `socket.to()`

### Endpoint
N/A (Method call)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body

- **event** (string) - Required - The name of the event to emit.
- **args** (any[]) - Optional - The arguments to pass with the event.

### Socket.IO with Redis Adapter (ioredis package, cluster)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Integrates Socket.IO with the Redis adapter using the 'ioredis' package and a Redis cluster setup. This provides a robust solution for scaling Socket.IO applications.

```javascript
import { Cluster } from "ioredis";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-adapter";  
  
const pubClient = new Cluster([  
  {  
    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7000,  
  },  
  {  
    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7001,  
  },  
  {  
    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7002,  
  },  
]);  
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();  
  
const io = new Server({  
  adapter: createAdapter(pubClient, subClient)  
});  
  
io.listen(3000);  


```

### Standalone Socket.IO Server Initialization

const options = { /* ... */ };

const options = { /* ... */ };

### Main Namespace Operations (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Illustrates that the main Socket.IO namespace ('/') can be accessed directly via the `io` instance. This example shows equivalent methods for handling connections, using middleware, and emitting events for the main namespace.

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {});
io.use((socket, next) => { next() });
io.emit("hello");
// are actually equivalent to
io.of("/").on("connection", (socket) => {});
io.of("/").use((socket, next) => { next() });
io.of("/").emit("hello");
```

### Configure Socket.IO Server with Redis Adapter (ES Modules)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-options

Shows how to set up the Socket.IO server with the Redis adapter using ES module syntax. This is useful for modern JavaScript projects. It involves importing necessary modules from 'socket.io', '@socket.io/redis-adapter', and 'redis', then initializing Redis clients for publishing and subscribing.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-adapter";
import { createClient } from "redis";

const pubClient = createClient({ host: "localhost", port: 6379 });
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();

const io = new Server({
  adapter: createAdapter(pubClient, subClient)
});

io.listen(3000);
```

### Server Instantiation with HTTP Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Demonstrates how to create a Socket.IO server instance bound to an existing Node.js HTTP server.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io

### Description
Instantiates a new Socket.IO server and binds it to an existing HTTP server.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/websites/socket_io

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **httpServer** (http.Server) - Required - The HTTP server instance to which the Socket.IO server will be attached.
- **options** (Object) - Optional - Configuration options for the Socket.IO server.
  - **path** (string) - Default: `/socket.io` - The namespace path for Socket.IO connections.
  - **serveClient** (boolean) - Default: `true` - Whether to serve the client-side files.
  - **adapter** (Object) - The adapter to use for broadcasting messages. Defaults to a memory-based adapter.
  - **origins** (string) - Default: `*` - Allowed origins for client connections.
  - **parser** (Object) - The parser to use for message serialization. Defaults to Socket.IO's built-in parser.
  - **pingTimeout** (number) - Default: `5000` - Milliseconds without a pong packet to consider the connection closed.
  - **pingInterval** (number) - Default: `25000` - Milliseconds before sending a new ping packet.
  - **upgradeTimeout** (number) - Default: `10000` - Milliseconds before an uncompleted transport upgrade is cancelled.
  - **maxHttpBufferSize** (number) - Default: `10e7` - Maximum bytes or characters a message can be before closing the session.
  - **allowRequest** (function) - A function to decide whether to continue a handshake or upgrade request.
  - **transports** (Array<string>) - Default: `['polling', 'websocket']` - Transports to allow connections to.
  - **allowUpgrades** (boolean) - Default: `true` - Whether to allow transport upgrades.
  - **perMessageDeflate** (boolean) - Default: `false` - Enable permessage-deflate extension for WebSocket.
  - **httpCompression** (boolean) - Default: `true` - Parameters for HTTP compression for polling transports.
  - **cookie** (string|boolean) - Default: `io` - Name of the HTTP cookie for client sid.
  - **cookiePath** (string) - Default: `/` - Path for the cookie option.
  - **cookieHttpOnly** (boolean) - Default: `true` - Whether the cookie is HttpOnly.
  - **wsEngine** (string) - Default: `ws` - WebSocket server implementation to use.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "httpServer": "<http.Server instance>",
  "options": {
    "path": "/test",
    "serveClient": false,
    "pingInterval": 10000,
    "pingTimeout": 5000,
    "cookie": false
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **io** (Object) - The instantiated Socket.IO server object.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "io": "<Socket.IO Server Instance>"
}
```
```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO Middleware for Authentication (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/migrating-from-0-9

Demonstrates how to use the `io.use()` method to implement custom authentication logic for incoming socket connections. This replaces the older `io.set('authorization')` method and provides a more structured way to handle connection authentication.

```javascript
var srv = require('http').createServer();
var io = require('socket.io')(srv);
var run = 0;

io.use(function(socket, next) {
  run++; // 0 -> 1
  next();
});

io.use(function(socket, next) {
  run++; // 1 -> 2
  next();
});

var socket = require('socket.io-client')();
socket.on('connect', function() {
  // run == 2 at this time
});
```

```javascript
io.set('authorization', function (handshakeData, callback) {
  // make sure the handshake data looks good
  callback(null, true); // error first, 'authorized' boolean second
});
```

```javascript
io.use(function(socket, next) {
  var handshakeData = socket.request;
  // make sure the handshake data looks good as before
  // if error do this:
    // next(new Error('not authorized'));
  // else just call next
  next();
});
```

```javascript
io.of('/namespace').use(function(socket, next) {
  var handshakeData = socket.request;
  next();
});
```

### Ping/Pong for Latency

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The 'pong' event for retrieving latency has been removed. Clients can now implement a custom 'ping' event handler on the server to measure latency.

```APIDOC
## Custom Ping/Pong for Latency Measurement

### Description
This endpoint allows for custom implementation of latency measurement using a 'ping' event between client and server. The 'pong' event is deprecated.

### Method
POST (for emitting 'ping') / ON (for listening 'ping')

### Endpoint
`socket.emit('ping', callback)` (client-side)
`socket.on('ping', callback)` (server-side)

### Parameters
#### Client-side `emit` Parameters
- **callback** (function) - Optional - A callback function to be executed upon receiving a response from the server, providing latency information.

#### Server-side `on` Parameters
- **callback** (function) - A function that will be called when the 'ping' event is received. It can optionally accept a callback to send back a response to the client.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Server-side
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.on("ping", (cb) => {
    if (typeof cb === "function")
      cb();
  });
});

// Client-side
setInterval(() => {
  const start = Date.now();
  // volatile, so the packet will be discarded if the socket is not connected
  socket.volatile.emit("ping", () => {
    const latency = Date.now() - start;
    console.log(`Latency: ${latency}ms`);
  });
}, 5000);
```

### Response
#### Success Response (via callback)
- The client-side callback receives the calculated latency in milliseconds.
```

### Socket.IO Custom Wait Function

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Implements a utility function `waitFor` to easily wait for a specific event to be emitted by a Socket.IO socket. This is useful for synchronizing asynchronous operations in tests.

```typescript
function waitFor(socket: ServerSocket | ClientSocket, event: string) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

// Usage example:
it("should work with waitFor()", () => {
  clientSocket.emit("baz");

  return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
});
```

--------------------------------

### Socket Management Utility Methods

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

Provides utility methods for managing socket connections, including joining rooms, leaving rooms, disconnecting sockets, and fetching socket instances.

```APIDOC
## Utility Methods for Socket Management

### Description
These methods allow for programmatic management of socket connections and their room memberships.

### Methods

1.  **`io.socketsJoin(rooms: string | string[])`**
    *   **Description**: Makes the matching socket instances join the specified rooms.
    *   **Example**: `io.socketsJoin("room1"); io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsJoin("room2");`

2.  **`io.socketsLeave(rooms: string | string[])`**
    *   **Description**: Makes the matching socket instances leave the specified rooms.
    *   **Example**: `io.socketsLeave("room1"); io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsLeave("room2");`

3.  **`io.disconnectSockets(options?: { buildings?: string[], except?: string | string[] })`**
    *   **Description**: Makes the matching socket instances disconnect.
    *   **Example**: `io.disconnectSockets(); io.of("/admin").in("room1").disconnectSockets(); io.of("/admin").in(theSocketId).disconnectSockets(); io.of("/admin").in("room1").except("room2").local.disconnectSockets();`

4.  **`io.fetchSockets(options?: { buildings?: string[], except?: string | string[] }): Promise<Socket[]>`**
    *   **Description**: Returns the matching socket instances.
    *   **Example**: `const sockets = await io.fetchSockets(); const sockets = await io.of("/admin").in("room1").fetchSockets(); const sockets = await io.in(theSocketId).fetchSockets();`

### Parameters for Filters (apply to `in`, `except`, `local`)
*   **`rooms`**: A room name or an array of room names.
*   **`namespace`**: The namespace to target (e.g., `/admin`).
*   **`except`**: Excludes sockets in the specified rooms.
*   **`local`**: Limits the operation to the current Socket.IO server instance.

### Socket Object Properties
When `fetchSockets` is used, the returned `Socket` objects have the following properties:
*   `id` (string): The unique ID of the socket.
*   `handshake` (object): The handshake details.
*   `rooms` (Set<string>): A set of rooms the socket is currently in.

### Request Example (Broadcasting with filters)
```javascript
// Disconnect all sockets in the "admin" namespace that are in "room1" but not in "room2"
io.of("/admin").in("room1").except("room2").local.disconnectSockets();
```

### Response Example (Fetching Sockets)
```javascript
// Get all sockets in the "admin" namespace that are in "room1"
const sockets = await io.of("/admin").in("room1").fetchSockets();

for (const socket of sockets) {
  console.log(socket.id);
  // Can also emit, join, leave, disconnect from these socket objects
  socket.emit("data", "Some data");
}
```
```

### Socket.IO Namespace Connection with Payload Example (Text)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Presents the text-based packet format for connecting to a Socket.IO namespace with an additional payload, such as authentication tokens. The client sends a CONNECT packet with data, and the server responds with the session ID.

```text
Client > { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/admin", data: { "token": "123" } }  
Server > { type: CONNECT, namespace: "/admin", data: { sid: "iLnRaVGHY4B75TeVAAAB" } }  
```

### Socket.IO Polling - Namespace Connection Approval

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

A GET request following the namespace connection request. The server acknowledges the connection and may provide updated session information or confirmations.

```APIDOC
## GET /socket.io/

### Description
This GET request is part of the namespace connection process, where the server acknowledges the connection request and may provide updated session information.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/socket.io/

### Query Parameters
- **EIO** (string) - Required - The Engine.IO protocol version (e.g., "4").
- **transport** (string) - Required - The transport type, "polling" for this example.
- **t** (string) - Required - A timestamp to prevent browser caching.
- **sid** (string) - Required - The session ID.

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **Engine.IO Packet** (string) - Contains Engine.IO "message" (type 4) and Socket.IO "namespace connection" details.

#### Response Example
```json
40{"sid":"wZX3oN0bSVIhsaknAAAI"}
```
```

### Add Custom Query Parameters to Socket.IO Connection

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

Enables sending additional query parameters with the Socket.IO connection, accessible on the server via `socket.handshake.query`. These parameters cannot be updated after the session starts.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  query: {
    x: 42
  }
});
```

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log(socket.handshake.query); // prints { x: "42", EIO: "4", transport: "polling" }
});
```

```javascript
socket.io.on("reconnect_attempt", () => {
  socket.io.opts.query.x++;
});
```

--------------------------------

### Notable Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-initialization

Configuration options for the Socket.IO server, including perMessageDeflate and maxHttpBufferSize.

```APIDOC
## Notable Options

### `perMessageDeflate` option

*   **Default value:** `false`
*   **Description:** Enables or disables the permessage-deflate WebSocket extension for message compression. It's disabled by default in v2.4.0+ due to performance and memory overhead. To enable it (for older versions or specific needs):
    ```javascript
    const io = require('socket.io')({
      perMessageDeflate: false // Set to true to enable if needed
    });
    ```

### `maxHttpBufferSize` option

*   **Default value:** `1e6` (1MB)
*   **Description:** Sets the maximum size in bytes allowed for a single message before the socket is closed. This can be increased or decreased based on application requirements.
    ```javascript
    const io = require('socket.io')({
      maxHttpBufferSize: 1e8 // Example: set to 100MB
    });
    ```
    This option corresponds to the `maxPayload` option of the underlying `ws` package.
```

### Test Cleanup for Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Ensures proper cleanup of Socket.IO resources after tests are completed. This involves closing the Socket.IO server and disconnecting the client socket to prevent resource leaks.

```javascript
afterAll(() => {
  io.close();
  clientSocket.disconnect();
});
```

```javascript
test.onFinish(() => {
  io.close();
  clientSocket.disconnect();
});
```

```typescript
test.onFinish(() => {
  io.close();
  clientSocket.disconnect();
});
```

### Socket.IO 2.x Event Emission Examples

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/emit-cheatsheet

Demonstrates various ways to emit events using Socket.IO in JavaScript. This includes sending to a single client, broadcasting to all clients, sending to specific rooms, and using options like acknowledgements and compression. Note that Socket.IO 2.x is no longer actively maintained.

```javascript
io.on('connection', onConnect);

function onConnect(socket){
  
  // sending to the client
  socket.emit('hello', 'can you hear me?', 1, 2, 'abc');
  
  // sending to all clients except sender
  socket.broadcast.emit('broadcast', 'hello friends!');
  
  // sending to all clients in 'game' room except sender
  socket.to('game').emit('nice game', "let's play a game");
  
  // sending to all clients in 'game1' and/or in 'game2' room, except sender
  socket.to('game1').to('game2').emit('nice game', "let's play a game (too)");
  
  // sending to all clients in 'game' room, including sender
  io.in('game').emit('big-announcement', 'the game will start soon');
  
  // sending to all clients in namespace 'myNamespace', including sender
  io.of('myNamespace').emit('bigger-announcement', 'the tournament will start soon');
  
  // sending to a specific room in a specific namespace, including sender
  io.of('myNamespace').to('room').emit('event', 'message');
  
  // sending to individual socketid (private message)
  io.to(socketId).emit('hey', 'I just met you');
  
  // WARNING: `socket.to(socket.id).emit()` will NOT work, as it will send to everyone in the room
  // named `socket.id` but the sender. Please use the classic `socket.emit()` instead.
  
  // sending with acknowledgement
  socket.emit('question', 'do you think so?', function (answer) {});
  
  // sending without compression
  socket.compress(false).emit('uncompressed', "that's rough");
  
  // sending a message that might be dropped if the client is not ready to receive messages
  socket.volatile.emit('maybe', 'do you really need it?');
  
  // specifying whether the data to send has binary data
  socket.binary(false).emit('what', 'I have no binaries!');
  
  // sending to all clients on this node (when using multiple nodes)
  io.local.emit('hi', 'my lovely babies');
  
  // sending to all connected clients
  io.emit('an event sent to all connected clients');
  
}
```

### Emit with Acknowledgement Test (Socket.IO)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

  t.plan(1);
  serverSocket.on("foo", (cb) => {
    cb("bar");
  });

  t.equal(result, "bar");
});

### Dynamically Create Namespaces for Tenants

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/namespaces

Illustrates how to dynamically create namespaces based on a regular expression, suitable for multi-tenant applications. Each namespace can emit events, and middleware can be applied to all dynamically created namespaces.

```javascript
const workspaces = io.of(/^\/\w+$/);

workspaces.on('connection', socket => {
  const workspace = socket.nsp;

  workspace.emit('hello');
});

// this middleware will be assigned to each namespace
workspaces.use((socket, next) => {
  // ensure the user has access to the workspace
  next();
});
```

This JavaScript code snippet configures the Socket.IO server to use a custom path for its connections, matching the reverse proxy setup.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server({
  path: "/my-custom-path/"
});
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with Port and Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Initializes a Socket.IO server by providing a port number and an options object. This creates a new http.Server internally. The options include Socket.IO specific settings like `path` and `serveClient`, as well as Engine.IO options such as `pingInterval` and `pingTimeout`.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(3000, {
  path: "/test",
  serveClient: false,
  // below are engine.IO options
  pingInterval: 10000,
  pingTimeout: 5000,
  cookie: false
});
```

### Configure Socket.IO Cookie Options (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-options

This example demonstrates how to configure cookie options for Socket.IO, such as name, httpOnly status, sameSite policy, and maxAge. Note that since Socket.IO v3, no cookie is sent by default.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  cookie: {
    name: "my-cookie",
    httpOnly: true,
    sameSite: "strict",
    maxAge: 86400
  }
});
```

### Customize Initial Request Headers - Node.js

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Listens for the 'initial_headers' event on the engine.IO server to customize response headers for the initial handshake request. This example shows setting a 'set-cookie' header.

```javascript
import { serialize } from "cookie";  
  
io.engine.on("initial_headers", (headers, request) => {  
  headers["set-cookie"] = serialize("uid", "1234", { sameSite: "strict" });  
});  

```

### JavaScript Socket.IO Server Configuration and Usage

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

A reference configuration for a Node.js Socket.IO server implementation, demonstrating setup with custom ping intervals, timeouts, maximum payload size, CORS origins, and event handling for connection, messages, and acknowledgments. It also shows how to handle custom namespaces.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server(3000, {
  pingInterval: 300,
  pingTimeout: 200,
  maxPayload: 1000000,
  cors: {
    origin: "*"
  }
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.emit("auth", socket.handshake.auth);

  socket.on("message", (...args) => {
    socket.emit.apply(socket, ["message-back", ...args]);
  });

  socket.on("message-with-ack", (...args) => {
    const ack = args.pop();
    ack(...args);
  })
});

io.of("/custom").on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.emit("auth", socket.handshake.auth);
});
```

--------------------------------

### Catch-all Event Listeners with `onAny` and `prependAny`

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Demonstrates the new catch-all listener functionality for both server and client sockets. `onAny` registers a listener for all events, while `prependAny` adds a listener to the beginning of the list. `offAny` removes listeners, and `listenersAny` retrieves them.

```javascript
// server  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  socket.onAny((event, ...args) => {});  
  socket.prependAny((event, ...args) => {});  
  socket.offAny(); // remove all listeners  
  socket.offAny(listener);  
  const listeners = socket.listenersAny();  
});  

// client  
const socket = io();  
socket.onAny((event, ...args) => {});  
socket.prependAny((event, ...args) => {});  
socket.offAny(); // remove all listeners  
socket.offAny(listener);  
const listeners = socket.listenersAny();  
```

### Socket.IO Protocol v5 CONNECT Packet with Payload (JSON Example)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Illustrates the structure of a CONNECT packet in Socket.IO protocol v5, which can include a JSON payload for authentication or authorization purposes. This demonstrates how clients can send tokens or other credentials.

```json
{
  "type": 0,
  "nsp": "/admin",
  "data": {
    "token": "123"
  }
}
```

### Initialize Socket.IO with MongoDB adapter (Capped Collection)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/mongo-adapter

Initializes a Socket.IO server and configures the MongoDB adapter using a capped collection for storing events. This setup requires a MongoDB replica set or sharded cluster.

```typescript
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/mongo-adapter";  
import { MongoClient } from "mongodb";  
  
const DB = "mydb";  
const COLLECTION = "socket.io-adapter-events";  
  
const io = new Server();  
  
const mongoClient = new MongoClient("mongodb://localhost:27017/?replicaSet=rs0");  
  
await mongoClient.connect();  
  
try {  
  await mongoClient.db(DB).createCollection(COLLECTION, {  
    capped: true,  
    size: 1e6  
  });  
} catch (e) {  
  // collection already exists  
}  
const mongoCollection = mongoClient.db(DB).collection(COLLECTION);  
  
io.adapter(createAdapter(mongoCollection));  
io.listen(3000);  

```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO WebSocket - Upgrade

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/socket-io-protocol

Illustrates the process of upgrading the connection from polling to WebSocket. This involves an initial GET request with the WebSocket transport, followed by Engine.IO probe requests/responses and Socket.IO messages over the WebSocket connection.

```APIDOC
## GET /socket.io/

### Description
Requests to upgrade the connection from polling to WebSocket. The server responds with a "101 Switching Protocols" status if the upgrade is successful.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/socket.io/

### Query Parameters
- **EIO** (string) - Required - The Engine.IO protocol version (e.g., "4").
- **transport** (string) - Required - The transport type, "websocket" for this upgrade request.
- **sid** (string) - Required - The session ID.

### Response
#### Success Response (101)
- **Switching Protocols** - Indicates the successful transition to the WebSocket protocol.

### WebSocket Frames
- **Client to Server**:
  - `2probe` - Engine.IO probe request.
  - `5` - Engine.IO "upgrade" packet type.
  - `42["hello"]` - Socket.IO "EVENT" packet.
  - `42["world"]` - Socket.IO "EVENT" packet.
  - `40/admin,` - Socket.IO "CONNECT" packet for the admin namespace.
  - `42/admin,1["tellme"]` - Socket.IO "EVENT" packet with acknowledgement.
  - `2` - Engine.IO "ping" packet type.
  - `1` - Engine.IO "close" packet type.

- **Server to Client**:
  - `3probe` - Engine.IO probe response.
  - `40/admin,{"sid":"-G5j-67EZFp-q59rADQM"}` - Grant access to the admin namespace.
  - `461-/admin,1[{"_placeholder":true,"num":0}]` - Socket.IO "BINARY_ACK" packet with a placeholder.
  - `<binary>` - Binary attachment data.
  - `3` - Engine.IO "pong" packet type.
```

### Fetching Sockets with `fetchSockets`

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

The `fetchSockets` utility method returns an array of matching socket instances. This method can be used to retrieve all sockets or filter them by namespace, room, or socket ID.

```javascript
// return all Socket instances
const sockets = await io.fetchSockets();

// return all Socket instances of the "admin" namespace in the "room1" room
const sockets = await io.of("/admin").in("room1").fetchSockets();

// this also works with a single socket ID
const sockets = await io.in(theSocketId).fetchSockets();
```

### Broadcasting to Multiple Rooms with `io.to()`

In Socket.IO v4, the `io.to()` and `socket.to()` methods now accept an array of room names, simplifying broadcasting to multiple rooms. This replaces the older loop-based approach.

```javascript
io.to(["room1", "room2", "room3"]).emit(/* ... */);

socket.to(["room1", "room2", "room3"]).emit(/* ... */);
```

### Close Socket.IO Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Provides an example of how to close a running Socket.IO server instance. The optional callback function is executed once all active connections have been terminated. This is useful for graceful shutdown procedures.

```javascript
const Server = require('socket.io');
const PORT   = 3030;
const server = require('http').Server();

const io = Server(PORT);

io.close(); // Close current server

server.listen(PORT); // PORT is free to use

io = Server(server);
```

### Initialize Socket.IO with Azure Service Bus Adapter (JavaScript)

Demonstrates how to set up a Socket.IO server with the Azure Service Bus adapter. It requires initializing Service Bus clients and passing them to the `createAdapter` function. The adapter is then used to initialize the Socket.IO server.

```javascript
import { ServiceBusClient, ServiceBusAdministrationClient } from "@azure/service-bus";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/azure-service-bus-adapter";  
  
const connectionString = "Endpoint=...";

  adapter: createAdapter(serviceBusClient, serviceBusAdminClient)
});

await io.of("/").adapter.init();  
  
io.listen(3000);  
```

### Custom waitFor Utility for Event Listening (Socket.IO)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/testing

Implements and utilizes a `waitFor` utility function that returns a promise resolving when a specific event is received on a given socket. This is useful for synchronizing asynchronous operations in tests.

```javascript
function waitFor(socket, event) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

test("it works with waitFor()", () => {
  clientSocket.emit("baz");

  return waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
});
```

```javascript
function waitFor(socket, event) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

test("it works with waitFor()", async (t) => {
  t.plan(1);
  clientSocket.emit("baz");

  await waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
  t.pass();
});
```

```typescript
function waitFor(socket: ServerSocket | ClientSocket, event: string) {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    socket.once(event, resolve);
  });
}

test("it works with waitFor()", async (t) => {
  t.plan(1);
  clientSocket.emit("baz");

  await waitFor(serverSocket, "baz");
  t.pass();
});
```

### Create Dynamic Namespaces with RegExp and Function

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Demonstrates creating Socket.IO namespaces dynamically using a regular expression to match pathnames or a function to conditionally create namespaces based on query parameters. It also shows how to broadcast events and use middleware within these dynamic namespaces.

```javascript
const dynamicNsp = io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/).on('connection', (socket) => {
  const newNamespace = socket.nsp; // newNamespace.name === '/dynamic-101'
  
  // broadcast to all clients in the given sub-namespace
  newNamespace.emit('hello');
});

// client-side
const socket = io('/dynamic-101');

// broadcast to all clients in each sub-namespace
dynamicNsp.emit('hello');

// use a middleware for each sub-namespace
dynamicNsp.use((socket, next) => { /* ... */ });
```

```javascript
io.of((name, query, next) => {
  // the checkToken method must return a boolean, indicating whether the client is able to connect or not.
  next(null, checkToken(query.token));
}).on('connection', (socket) => { /* ... */ });
```

### TypeScript Typed Events - Server-Side

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-3-x-to-4-0

On the server-side, TypeScript users can define event signatures for both client-to-server and server-to-client communications, enabling type checking for event handlers and emissions.

```typescript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

interface ClientToServerEvents {
  noArg: () => void;
  basicEmit: (a: number, b: string, c: number[]) => void;
}

interface ServerToClientEvents {
  withAck: (d: string, cb: (e: number) => void) => void;
}

const io = new Server<ClientToServerEvents, ServerToClientEvents>(3000);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
    socket.on("noArg", () => {
      // ...
    });

    socket.on("basicEmit", (a, b, c) => {
      // ...
    });

    socket.emit("withAck", "42", (e) => {
        console.log(e);
    });
});
```

const Server = require("socket.io");

// Optionally, pass options: io.attach(PORT, { ...options });
```

### Socket.IO 'connect_error' Event Handling and Reconnection

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-socket-instance

Demonstrates handling the 'connect_error' event, which occurs when the low-level connection fails or is denied by the server. It includes examples of updating authentication credentials and manually initiating reconnection.

```javascript
// Example 1: Updating auth token and reconnecting
socket.on("connect_error", () => {
  socket.auth.token = "abcd";
  socket.connect();
});

// Example 2: Reconnecting after a delay when auth is a function
const socket = io({
  auth: (cb) => {
    cb(localStorage.getItem("token"));
  }
});

socket.on("connect_error", () => {
  setTimeout(() => {
    socket.connect();
  }, 1000);
});
```

### Setup Clustered Socket.IO Server with SQLite (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/ending-notes

This CommonJS code snippet sets up a clustered Node.js server using Socket.IO for real-time communication. It initializes a SQLite database, creates a 'messages' table if it doesn't exist, and configures the server to handle connections and messages. It utilizes the `@socket.io/cluster-adapter` for efficient scaling across multiple CPU cores.

```javascript
const express = require('express');
const { createServer } = require('node:http');
const { join } = require('node:path');
const { Server } = require('socket.io');
const sqlite3 = require('sqlite3');
const { open } = require('sqlite');
const { availableParallelism } = require('node:os');
const cluster = require('node:cluster');
const { createAdapter, setupPrimary } = require('@socket.io/cluster-adapter');

if (cluster.isPrimary) {
  const numCPUs = availableParallelism();
  for (let i = 0; i < numCPUs; i++) {
    cluster.fork({
      PORT: 3000 + i
    });
  }

  return setupPrimary();
}

async function main() {
  const db = await open({
    filename: 'chat.db',
    driver: sqlite3.Database
  });

  await db.exec(`
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (
      id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
      client_offset TEXT UNIQUE,
      content TEXT
    );
  `);

  const app = express();
  const server = createServer(app);
  const io = new Server(server, {
    connectionStateRecovery: {},
    adapter: createAdapter()
  });

  app.get('/', (req, res) => {
    res.sendFile(join(__dirname, 'index.html'));
  });

  io.on('connection', async (socket) => {
    socket.on('chat message', async (msg, clientOffset, callback) => {
      let result;
      try {
        result = await db.run('INSERT INTO messages (content, client_offset) VALUES (?, ?)', msg, clientOffset);
      } catch (e) {
        if (e.errno === 19 /* SQLITE_CONSTRAINT */ ) {
          callback();
        } else {
          // nothing to do, just let the client retry
        }
        return;
      }
      io.emit('chat message', msg, result.lastID);
      callback();
    });

    if (!socket.recovered) {
      try {
        await db.each('SELECT id, content FROM messages WHERE id > ?',
          [socket.handshake.auth.serverOffset || 0],
          (_err, row) => {
            socket.emit('chat message', row.content, row.id);
          }
        )
      } catch (e) {
        // something went wrong
      }
    }
  });

  const port = process.env.PORT;

  server.listen(port, () => {
    console.log(`server running at http://localhost:${port}`);
  });
}

main();

```

### Replace Socket.use() with Socket.onAny() in Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The Socket.use() method in Socket.IO, which acted as a catch-all listener, has been replaced by socket.onAny(). The new method provides a more intuitive API for handling any event emitted by a socket.

```javascript
socket.use((packet, next) => {
  console.log(packet.data);
  next();
});

```

```javascript
socket.onAny((event, ...args) => {
  console.log(event);
});

```

### Client-Side Sending Credentials to Socket.IO Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/middlewares

Provides examples of how a Socket.IO client can send authentication credentials to the server using the `auth` option. Credentials can be a plain object or provided via a callback function, allowing for dynamic token generation.

```javascript
// plain object
const socket = io({
  auth: {
    token: "abc"
  }
});

// or with a function
const socket = io({
  auth: (cb) => {
    cb({
      token: "abc"
    });
  }
});
```

--------------------------------

### Room Name Coercion

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Room names are no longer implicitly coerced to strings. Socket.IO now uses Maps and Sets internally, requiring explicit type matching for room names.

```APIDOC
## Room Name Type Handling

### Description
Explains the change in room name handling, where implicit string coercion is removed. Room names must now match the type used when joining.

### Method
`socket.join(roomName)` and `io.to(roomName)`

### Endpoint
N/A

### Parameters
#### `join` Parameters
- **roomName** (string | number | any) - The name or identifier of the room to join.

#### `to` Parameters
- **roomName** (string | number | any) - The name or identifier of the room to target.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Before (implicit coercion worked)
// socket.join(42);
// io.to("42").emit("hello");

// After (type must match)
// Option 1: Using string
socket.join("42");
io.to("42").emit("hello");

// Option 2: Using number
socket.join(42);
io.to(42).emit("hello");
```
```

--------------------------------

### Migrate Socket.IO Redis Adapter (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Demonstrates the migration from the older 'socket.io-redis' package to the newer '@socket.io/redis-adapter' package in Node.js. This involves updating how Redis clients are created and passed to the adapter.

```javascript
const redisAdapter = require("socket.io-redis");

io.adapter(redisAdapter({ host: "localhost", port: 6379 }));
```

```javascript
const { createClient } = require("redis");
const { createAdapter } = require("@socket.io/redis-adapter");

const pubClient = createClient({ url: "redis://localhost:6379" });
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();

io.adapter(createAdapter(pubClient, subClient));
```

### Replace io.set('authorization') with io.use() in Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The io.set('authorization') method for custom authentication in Socket.IO has been removed and replaced by the io.use() middleware. This change impacts how handshake data is processed for authorization.

```javascript
io.set("authorization", (handshakeData, callback) => {
  // make sure the handshake data looks good
  callback(null, true); // error first, "authorized" boolean second
});

```

```javascript
io.use((socket, next) => {
  var handshakeData = socket.request;
  // make sure the handshake data looks good as before
  // if error do this:
    // next(new Error("not authorized"));
  // else just call next
  next();
});

```

```javascript
io.emit('hello', 'world');
```

### Register Event Handlers per File with Socket.IO 3.x

```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer);

const registerOrderHandlers = require("./orderHandler");
const registerUserHandlers = require("./userHandler");

const onConnection = (socket) => {
  registerOrderHandlers(io, socket);
  registerUserHandlers(io, socket);
}

io.on("connection", onConnection);

```

```javascript
module.exports = (io, socket) => {
  const createOrder = (payload) => {
    // ...
  }

  const readOrder = (orderId, callback) => {
    // ...
  }

  socket.on("order:create", createOrder);
  socket.on("order:read", readOrder);
}
```

### Configure Socket.IO Server and Client with Self-Signed Certificate

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-api

This example shows how to set up a secure Socket.IO connection using a self-signed SSL certificate. It includes server-side code for creating an HTTPS server and client-side code for configuring the connection to trust the certificate, with options for providing the CA certificate or disabling certificate verification (with a security warning).

```javascript
// server-side
const fs = require('fs');
const server = require('https').createServer({
  key: fs.readFileSync('server-key.pem'),
  cert: fs.readFileSync('server-cert.pem')
});
const io = require('socket.io')(server);
server.listen(3000);

// client-side
const socket = io({
  // option 1
  ca: fs.readFileSync('server-cert.pem'),

  // option 2. WARNING: it leaves you vulnerable to MITM attacks!
  rejectUnauthorized: false
});
```

const options = { /* ... */ };

### Client and Server Authentication with Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

Demonstrates how to configure authentication credentials on the client-side using a token or a callback function, and how the server accesses these credentials. Also shows how to update credentials and reconnect upon connection errors.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  auth: {
    token: "abcd"
  }
});

// or with a function
const socket = io({
  auth: (cb) => {
    cb({ token: localStorage.token })
  }
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log(socket.handshake.auth); // prints { token: "abcd" }
});

socket.on("connect_error", (err) => {
  if (err.message === "invalid credentials") {
    socket.auth.token = "efgh";
    socket.connect();
  }
});

socket.auth.token = "efgh";
socket.disconnect().connect();
```

### General Connection Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Configure core aspects of the Socket.IO client connection, such as credential handling, binary encoding, and request timestamping.

```APIDOC
## General Connection Options

### `withCredentials`

*   **Description**: Whether or not cross-site requests should be made using credentials such as cookies, authorization headers or TLS client certificates. Setting `withCredentials` has no effect on same-site requests.
*   **Default Value**: `false`
*   **Type**: `boolean`

### `forceBase64`

*   **Description**: Whether to force base64 encoding for binary content sent over WebSocket (always enabled for HTTP long-polling).
*   **Default Value**: `false`
*   **Type**: `boolean`

### `timestampRequests`

*   **Description**: Whether to add the timestamp query param to each request (for cache busting).
*   **Default Value**: `true`
*   **Type**: `boolean`

### `timestampParam`

*   **Description**: The name of the query parameter to use as our timestamp key.
*   **Default Value**: `"t"`
*   **Type**: `string`
```

### Default Adapter Configuration

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Configuration options for the default Socket.IO Redis adapter.

```APIDOC
## Default Adapter Configuration

### Description
Configuration options for the default Socket.IO Redis adapter.

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **key** (string) - Optional - The prefix for the Redis Pub/Sub channels. Defaults to `socket.io`.
- **requestsTimeout** (number) - Optional - After this timeout the adapter will stop waiting for responses to requests. Defaults to `5000`.
- **publishOnSpecificResponseChannel** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to publish a response to the channel specific to the requesting node. Defaults to `false`.
- **parser** (object) - Optional - The parser to use for encoding and decoding messages sent to Redis. Defaults to `-`.

### Tip
Setting the `publishOnSpecificResponseChannel` option to `true` is more efficient since the responses are only sent to the requesting server. However, it defaults to `false` for backward-compatibility.
```

```javascript
import { Cluster } from "ioredis";

    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7000,  
  },  
  {  
    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7001,  
  },  
  {  
    host: "localhost",  
    port: 7002,  
  },  
]);  
  
const io = new Server({  
  adapter: createAdapter(redisClient)  
});

```

### Socket.IO Cluster Setup with CommonJS

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-9

Sets up a horizontally scalable Socket.IO server using Node.js's built-in `cluster` module and the `@socket.io/cluster-adapter`. This code configures the primary process to spawn workers for each CPU core and sets up the adapter for inter-worker communication.

```javascript
const express = require('express');  
const { createServer } = require('node:http');  
const { join } = require('node:path');  
const { Server } = require('socket.io');  
const sqlite3 = require('sqlite3');  
const { open } = require('sqlite');  
const { availableParallelism } = require('node:os');  
const cluster = require('node:cluster');  
const { createAdapter, setupPrimary } = require('@socket.io/cluster-adapter');  
  
if (cluster.isPrimary) {  
  const numCPUs = availableParallelism();  
  // create one worker per available core  
  for (let i = 0; i < numCPUs; i++) {  
    cluster.fork({  
      PORT: 3000 + i  
    });  
  }  
    
  // set up the adapter on the primary thread  
  return setupPrimary();  
}  
  
async function main() {  
  const app = express();  
  const server = createServer(app);  
  const io = new Server(server, {  
    connectionStateRecovery: {},  
    // set up the adapter on each worker thread  
    adapter: createAdapter()  
  });  
  
  // [...]  
  
  // each worker will listen on a distinct port  
  const port = process.env.PORT;  
  
  server.listen(port, () => {  
    console.log(`server running at http://localhost:${port}`);  
  });  
}  
  
main();  

```

### Emit Events Sequentially

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Shows the updated way to emit multiple events sequentially. The `emit()` method no longer supports chaining and returns a boolean indicating if the event was queued, so each `emit()` call must be on a separate line.

```javascript
socket.emit("event1");  
socket.emit("event2");  
```

### Use Socket.IO with Redis Streams Adapter and 'redis' cluster

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-streams-adapter

Shows how to set up Socket.IO with the Redis Streams adapter when using a Redis cluster with the 'redis' package. It configures a cluster client with root nodes and then initializes the Socket.IO server.

```javascript
import { createCluster } from "redis";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-streams-adapter";  
  
const redisClient = createCluster({  
  rootNodes: [  
    {  
      url: "redis://localhost:7000",  
    },  
    {  
      url: "redis://localhost:7001",  
    },  
    {  
      url: "redis://localhost:7002",  
    },  
  ],  
});  
  
await redisClient.connect();  
  
const io = new Server({  
  adapter: createAdapter(redisClient)  
});  
  
io.listen(3000);  

```

### Integrate Socket.IO with Express Session (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/faq

This code demonstrates how to integrate Socket.IO with express-session to manage user sessions across HTTP and WebSocket connections. It requires the 'express', 'express-session', and 'socket.io' packages. The example shows how to apply the session middleware to both Express and Socket.IO, and how to access session data within a Socket.IO connection.

```javascript
const express = require('express');  
const session = require('express-session');  
const app = express();  
  
const server = require('http').createServer(app);  
const io = require('socket.io')(server);  
  
const sessionMiddleware = session({ secret: 'keyboard cat', cookie: { maxAge: 60000 }});  
// register middleware in Express  
app.use(sessionMiddleware);  
// register middleware in Socket.IO  
io.use((socket, next) => {  
  sessionMiddleware(socket.request, {}, next);  
  // sessionMiddleware(socket.request, socket.request.res, next); will not work with websocket-only  
  // connections, as 'socket.request.res' will be undefined in that case  
});  
  
io.on('connection', (socket) => {  
  const session = socket.request.session;  
  session.connections++;  
  session.save();  
});  
  
const port = process.env.PORT || 3000;  
server.listen(port, () => console.log('server listening on port ' + port));  

```

### emit() Method Signature Change

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The `emit()` method's return value has changed from the current object to `true`, matching the `EventEmitter.emit()` signature. Chaining `emit()` calls is no longer supported.

```APIDOC
## Emit Method Behavior Change

### Description
Updates the behavior of the `emit()` method to return a boolean indicating success and to disallow chaining of `emit()` calls.

### Method
`emit()`

### Endpoint
N/A

### Parameters
#### `emit` Parameters
- **event** (string) - The name of the event to emit.
- **...args** - The arguments to pass to the event listeners.
- **callback** (function) - Optional - A callback function to be executed upon acknowledgment.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Before (chaining was possible)
// socket.emit("event1").emit("event2");

// After (chaining is not possible)
socket.emit("event1");
socket.emit("event2");
```

### Response
#### Success Response (return value)
- **true** (boolean) - Indicates that the event was emitted successfully.
```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO Direct Packet Decoding

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/migrating-from-0-9

Directly decodes a packet using the `parser.decode` method. This is suitable for simpler packet structures. It takes the encoded data as input and returns the fully formed packet.

```javascript
var packet = parser.decode(decoding);
console.log(packet); // formed socket.io packet to handle
```

  socket  
    .timeout(5000)

### Utility Methods for Socket Instance Management

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Methods for managing Socket instances, including joining/leaving rooms, disconnecting sockets, and fetching sockets based on criteria.

```APIDOC
## Utility Methods for Socket Instance Management

### Description
Utility methods added in Socket.IO v4.0.0 to manage Socket instances and their rooms. These methods provide granular control over socket connections and their room memberships, sharing semantics with broadcasting and compatible with the Redis adapter.

### Methods

#### `socketsJoin(rooms)`

##### Description
Makes the matching socket instances join the specified rooms.

##### Method
`io.socketsJoin(rooms)`

##### Endpoint
N/A (Instance method)

##### Parameters
- **rooms** (string or array of strings) - The room(s) to join.

##### Request Example
```javascript
// Make all Socket instances join the "room1" room
io.socketsJoin("room1");

// Make all Socket instances in the "room1" room join the "room2" and "room3" rooms
io.in("room1").socketsJoin(["room2", "room3"]);

// Make all Socket instances in the "admin" namespace's "room1" join the "room2" room
io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsJoin("room2");

// This also works with a single socket ID
io.in(theSocketId).socketsJoin("room1");
```

#### `socketsLeave(rooms)`

##### Description
Makes the matching socket instances leave the specified rooms.

##### Method
`io.socketsLeave(rooms)`

##### Endpoint
N/A (Instance method)

##### Parameters
- **rooms** (string or array of strings) - The room(s) to leave.

##### Request Example
```javascript
// Make all Socket instances leave the "room1" room
io.socketsLeave("room1");

// Make all Socket instances in the "room1" room leave the "room2" and "room3" rooms
io.in("room1").socketsLeave(["room2", "room3"]);

// Make all Socket instances in the "admin" namespace's "room1" leave the "room2" room
io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsLeave("room2");

// This also works with a single socket ID
io.in(theSocketId).socketsLeave("room1");
```

#### `disconnectSockets(close)`

##### Description
Makes the matching socket instances disconnect.

##### Method
`io.disconnectSockets(close)`

##### Endpoint
N/A (Instance method)

##### Parameters
- **close** (boolean, optional) - If true, also disconnects the low-level connection.

##### Request Example
```javascript
// Make all Socket instances disconnect
io.disconnectSockets();

// Make all Socket instances in the "room1" room disconnect (and discard the low-level connection)
io.in("room1").disconnectSockets(true);

// Make all Socket instances in the "admin" namespace's "room1" disconnect
io.of("/admin").in("room1").disconnectSockets();

// This also works with a single socket ID
io.of("/admin").in(theSocketId).disconnectSockets();
```

#### `fetchSockets(opts)`

##### Description
Returns the matching socket instances.

##### Method
`io.fetchSockets(opts)`

##### Endpoint
N/A (Instance method)

##### Parameters
- **opts** (object, optional) - Options to filter sockets. Supports `rooms` (string or array of strings) and `namespace` (string).

##### Request Example
```javascript
// Fetch all socket instances in the "room1" room
const sockets = await io.in("room1").fetchSockets();

// Fetch all socket instances in the "admin" namespace
const adminSockets = await io.of("/admin").fetchSockets();
```

### Cross-Server Compatibility
These methods are compatible with the Redis adapter (starting with `socket.io-redis@6.1.0`), allowing them to work across multiple Socket.IO servers.
```

--------------------------------

## Server Constructor

  // options

### Test Connection State Recovery with Client Reconnection

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/connection-state-recovery

A client-side example demonstrating how to test connection state recovery by intentionally closing the underlying Engine.IO connection to trigger a reconnection. It logs whether the recovery was successful.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  reconnectionDelay: 10000, // defaults to 1000
  reconnectionDelayMax: 10000 // defaults to 5000
});

socket.on("connect", () => {
  console.log("recovered?", socket.recovered);

  setTimeout(() => {
    if (socket.io.engine) {
      // close the low-level connection and trigger a reconnection
      socket.io.engine.close();
    }
  }, 10000);
});

```

### Socket.IO Middleware with Connection State Recovery

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-options

Example of a Socket.IO middleware that is potentially skipped during connection state recovery. This middleware customizes socket data based on headers. If `skipMiddlewares` is true in `connectionStateRecovery`, this middleware will not run on successful recovery.

```javascript
function computeUserIdFromHeaders(headers) {
  // to be implemented
}

// this middleware will be skipped if the connection is successfully recovered
io.use(async (socket, next) => {
  socket.data.userId = await computeUserIdFromHeaders(socket.handshake.headers);

  next();
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // the userId attribute will either come:
  // - from the middleware above (first connection or failed recovery)
  // - from the recovery mechanism
  console.log("userId", socket.data.userId);
});
```

### Emit Volatile Events from Client

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Allows emitting events that may be dropped if the underlying transport is not ready. This is useful for client-side scenarios where the socket might not be connected, preventing message buffering until reconnection.

```javascript
socket.volatile.emit("volatile event", "might or might not be sent");  
```

### Import Socket.IO Client (JavaScript)

### Enable v2 Client Compatibility on Socket.IO Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Configures the Socket.IO server to allow connections from v2 clients during an upgrade process. The `allowEIO3` option must be set to `true` to maintain compatibility with older clients before they are updated.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")({  
  allowEIO3: true // false by default  
});  
```

### Client Emitting Volatile Events Periodically in Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/emitting-events

Illustrates a client emitting volatile 'ping' events at intervals using `setInterval`. This example shows how volatile events can be used for high-frequency updates where only the latest data matters.

```javascript
let count = 0;
setInterval(() => {
  socket.volatile.emit("ping", ++count);
}, 1000);
```

### Configure CORS for Socket.IO with Credentials (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

This Node.js example demonstrates the server-side configuration for Socket.IO to allow cross-origin requests with credentials. It involves setting up the `http` server and Socket.IO instance with a `cors` object that specifies the allowed origin and enables credential handling.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http"; 
import { Server } from "socket.io"; 

const httpServer = createServer(); 
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  cors: {
    origin: "https://my-frontend.com",
    credentials: true
  }
});
```

### Configure Socket.IO Server with Msgpack Parser

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Integrates the socket.io-msgpack-parser for more efficient handling of binary content and smaller payloads. This parser improves performance by sending binary events in a single WebSocket frame and is available via CDN or server. Note the lack of IE9 support.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {  
  parser: require("socket.io-msgpack-parser")  
});  
```
