### Socket.IO Server-Side 'At Least Once' Delivery (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/delivery-guarantees

Implements server-side 'at least once' delivery for Socket.IO events. This involves assigning unique IDs to events, persisting them to a database, and having the client track the last received event's offset. Upon reconnection, the server can resend any missed events based on this offset. This example shows the server logic for handling reconnections and emitting events.

```javascript
io.on("connection", async (socket) => {
  const offset = socket.handshake.auth.offset;
  if (offset) {
    // this is a reconnection
    for (const event of await fetchMissedEventsFromDatabase(offset)) {
      socket.emit("my-event", event);
    }
  } else {
    // this is a first connection
  }
});

setInterval(async () => {
  const event = {
    id: generateUniqueId(),
    data: new Date().toISOString()
  }

  await persistEventToDatabase(event);
  io.emit("my-event", event);
}, 1000);
```

This code configures the Socket.IO server to handle Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS) by explicitly allowing requests from a specified origin and enabling credential handling. This is necessary for Socket.IO v3 and later when the frontend is not served from the same domain as the backend, ensuring secure communication.

  }

### Handling Connection Errors and Reconnection Logic

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-socket-instance

Demonstrates how to handle `connect_error` events, distinguishing between temporary failures that allow automatic reconnection and server-denied connections. It also shows how to manually trigger reconnection if needed.

```javascript
socket.on("connect_error", (error) => {
  if (socket.active) {
    // temporary failure, the socket will automatically try to reconnect
  } else {
    // the connection was denied by the server
    // in that case, `socket.connect()` must be manually called in order to reconnect
    console.log(error.message);
  }
});
```

### Resolve TS2416 Property 'emit' Not Assignable in Socket.IO Namespace/Socket

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The TypeScript error TS2416, indicating that the 'emit' property is not assignable, has been resolved in Socket.IO version 3.0.1. This issue relates to changes in the method signature of `emit()` in newer versions. Updating to version 3.0.1 or later will fix this compatibility problem.

```typescript
node_modules/socket.io/dist/namespace.d.ts(89,5): error TS2416: Property 'emit' in type 'Namespace' is not assignable to the same property in base type 'EventEmitter'.
  Type '(ev: string, ...args: any[]) => Namespace' is not assignable to type '(event: string | symbol, ...args: any[]) => boolean'.
    Type 'Namespace' is not assignable to type 'boolean'.
node_modules/socket.io/dist/socket.d.ts(84,5): error TS2416: Property 'emit' in type 'Socket' is not assignable to the same property in base type 'EventEmitter'.
  Type '(ev: string, ...args: any[]) => this' is not assignable to type '(event: string | symbol, ...args: any[]) => boolean'.
    Type 'this' is not assignable to type 'boolean'.
      Type 'Socket' is not assignable to type 'boolean'.
```

--------------------------------

### Address 'packet.data is undefined' in Socket.IO Client-Server Mismatch

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

The 'Uncaught TypeError: packet.data is undefined' error typically occurs when a Socket.IO v3 client attempts to connect to a v2 server. This incompatibility prevents proper communication. Ensure that your client and server are using compatible versions of Socket.IO, preferably both being v3 or later.

```text
Uncaught TypeError: packet.data is undefined
```

--------------------------------

### Configure CORS in Socket.IO Server (v3.x)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0

Updates the configuration for Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS) in Socket.IO servers from version 3.x. It replaces the `origins` and `handlePreflightRequest` options with a unified `cors` object, which is forwarded to the 'cors' package. This change enhances security by disabling CORS by default and requiring explicit configuration.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  cors: {
    origin: "https://example.com",
    methods: ["GET", "POST"],
    allowedHeaders: ["my-custom-header"],
    credentials: true
  }
});
```

--------------------------------

### Creating Socket Instances with Separate Managers using forceNew (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

Illustrates how to force the creation of new Manager instances for each Socket instance by setting the `forceNew` option to `true`. This results in distinct connections for each Socket.

```javascript
const socket = io("https://example.com"); // the main namespace  
const productSocket = io("https://example.com/product", { forceNew: true }); // the "product" namespace  
const orderSocket = io("https://example.com/order", { forceNew: true }); // the "order" namespace  
```

--------------------------------

### Socket.handshake Object Structure and Usage (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Provides access to handshake details for an incoming Socket.IO connection. This object contains information like request headers, connection time, client IP address, cross-domain status, security status, timestamp, URL, query parameters, and authentication payload. It can be accessed during middleware setup or after a connection is established.

```javascript
io.use((socket, next) => {  
  let handshake = socket.handshake;  
  // ...  
});  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  let handshake = socket.handshake;  
  // ...  
});  
```

### Basic Event Emission (Client to Server)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/emitting-events

Illustrates how a client can emit an event to the server. The server listens for the 'hello' event and logs the received argument.

```javascript
// client-side  
socket.on("hello", (arg) => {  
  console.log(arg); 
});  
```

--------------------------------

### Usage with recluster (Master Process)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/cluster-adapter

Sets up the master process for using Socket.IO with the cluster adapter and the 'recluster' module. This configuration handles sticky sessions and worker communication, delegating worker process management to 'recluster'.

```javascript
const cluster = require("cluster");  
const http = require("http");  
const { setupMaster } = require("@socket.io/sticky");  
const { setupPrimary } = require("@socket.io/cluster-adapter");  
const recluster = require("recluster");  
const path = require("path");  
  
const httpServer = http.createServer();  
  
// setup sticky sessions  
setupMaster(httpServer, {  
  loadBalancingMethod: "least-connection",  
});  
  
// setup connections between the workers  
setupPrimary();  
  
// needed for packets containing buffers (you can ignore it if you only send plaintext objects)  
// Node.js < 16.0.0  
cluster.setupMaster({  
  serialization: "advanced",  
});  
// Node.js > 16.0.0  
// cluster.setupPrimary({  
//   serialization: "advanced",  
// });  
  
httpServer.listen(3000);  
  
const balancer = recluster(path.join(__dirname, "worker.js"));  
  
balancer.run();  

```

### Configure Node.js Server for Client Certificate Authentication (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Set up a Node.js HTTPS server to request and verify client certificates for authentication in Socket.IO connections. This requires configuring `requestCert` to `true` and providing the CA certificates used to sign the client certificates.

```javascript
const fs = require("fs");
const server = require("https").createServer({
  cert: fs.readFileSync("./server-cert.pem"),
  key: fs.readFileSync("./server-key.pem"),
  requestCert: true,
  ca: [
    fs.readFileSync('client-cert.pem')
  ]
});
const io = require("socket.io")(server);
```

```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  path: "/my-custom-path/"
});

### Configure Socket.IO Client Serving

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Explains how to control whether the Socket.IO server serves client files. This can be done during initialization by passing the `serveClient` option or by calling the `serveClient()` method before attaching the server. Note that this method has no effect after `attach` is called.

```javascript
// pass a server and the `serveClient` option
const io = require('socket.io')(http, { serveClient: false });

// or pass no server and then you can call the method
const io = require('socket.io')();
io.serveClient(false);
io.attach(http);
```

### Connect to Socket.IO using Manager (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-api

const { Manager } = require("socket.io-client");  
  
const manager = new Manager("ws://example.com", {  
  reconnectionDelayMax: 10000  
});  
  
const socket = manager.socket("/my-namespace", {  
  query: {  
    auth: "123"  
  }  
});  

```

### server.of

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Initializes and retrieves a Namespace by its pathname identifier. Supports string, RegExp, or function identifiers.

```APIDOC
## server.of(nsp)​

### Description
Initializes and retrieves the given `Namespace` by its pathname identifier `nsp`. If the namespace was already initialized it returns it immediately. A regex or a function can also be provided to create namespaces dynamically.

### Method
`server.of(nsp)`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
* **nsp** (String|RegExp|Function) - The namespace identifier (e.g., `/chat`) or a pattern/function to create dynamic namespaces.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Basic usage
const adminNamespace = io.of("/admin");

// Dynamic namespace with RegExp
const dynamicNsp = io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/).on("connection", (socket) => {
  const newNamespace = socket.nsp; // newNamespace.name === "/dynamic-101"
  newNamespace.emit("hello");
});

// Dynamic namespace with a function
io.of((name, query, next) => {
  // checkToken method must return a boolean
  next(null, checkToken(query.token));
}).on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
* **Namespace** - The Namespace object.

#### Response Example
```json
// Returns Namespace object
```
```

### Configure Engine.IO Client Options for Socket.IO Manager

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-api

Illustrates how to pass options specifically for the underlying Engine.IO client when initializing the Socket.IO Manager. These options control transport mechanisms, data encoding, and connection-specific settings like request timeouts and protocols.

```javascript
import { Manager } from "socket.io-client";

const manager = new Manager("https://example.com", {
  transports: ["websocket", "polling"],
  transportOptions: {
    polling: {
      requestTimeout: 10000
    }
  },
  upgrade: true,
  forceJSONP: false,
  jsonp: true,
  forceBase64: false,
  enablesXDR: false,
  timestampRequests: true,
  timestampParam: "_ts",
  rememberUpgrade: true,
  onlyBinaryUpgrades: false
});
```

### Handling Authentication Errors and Reconnection

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Provides strategies for handling authentication failures on the client, including updating credentials and reconnecting.

```APIDOC
## Handling Authentication Errors and Reconnection

### Description
Implement logic on the client to gracefully handle authentication errors received from the server. This includes updating credentials and initiating a reconnection attempt.

### Method
Client-side event listener and methods

### Parameters
#### `connect_error` Event Arguments
- **err** (Error) - An error object detailing the connection failure.
  - **err.message** (string) - The error message, e.g., "invalid credentials".

### Request Example (Updating Credentials and Reconnecting)
```javascript
socket.on("connect_error", (err) => {
  if (err.message === "invalid credentials") {
    socket.auth.token = "efgh";
    socket.connect();
  }
});
```

### Request Example (Manual Reconnection)
```javascript
socket.auth.token = "efgh";
socket.disconnect().connect();
```
```

### Server Methods

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Documentation for various methods available on the Socket.IO server instance.

```APIDOC
## Server Methods

### `server.adapter([value])`

  * `value` `<Adapter>`
  * **Returns** `<Server>` | `<Adapter>`

Sets the adapter `value`. Defaults to an instance of the `Adapter` that ships with socket.io which is memory based. See socket.io-adapter. If no arguments are supplied this method returns the current value.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-adapter";
import { createClient } from "redis";

const io = new Server();

const pubClient = createClient({ host: "localhost", port: 6379 });
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();

io.adapter(createAdapter(pubClient, subClient));

// redis@3
io.listen(3000);

// redis@4
Promise.all([pubClient.connect(), subClient.connect()]).then(() => {
  io.listen(3000);
});
```

### `server.attach(httpServer[, options])`

  * `httpServer` `<http.Server>` | `<https.Server>`
  * `options` `<Object>`

Attaches the `Server` to an `httpServer` with the supplied `options`.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer();
const io = new Server();

io.attach(httpServer);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);
```

### `server.attach(port[, options])`

  * `port` `<number>`
  * `options` `<Object>`

Attaches the `Server` on the given `port` with the supplied `options`.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server();

io.attach(3000);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});
```

### `server.attachApp(app[, options])`

  * `app` `<uws.App>`
  * `options` `<Object>`

Attaches the Socket.IO server to an µWebSockets.js app:

```javascript
import { App } from "uWebSockets.js";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const app = App();
const io = new Server();

io.attachApp(app);

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

app.listen(3000, (token) => {
  if (!token) {
    console.warn("port already in use");
  }
});
```

### `server.bind(engine)`

  * `engine` `<engine.Server>`
  * **Returns** `<Server>`

Advanced use only. Binds the server to a specific engine.io `Server` (or compatible API) instance.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { Server as Engine } from "engine.io";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer((req, res) => {
  res.writeHead(404).end();
});

const engine = new Engine();

engine.attach(httpServer, {
  path: "/socket.io/"
});

const io = new Server();

io.bind(engine);

httpServer.listen(3000);
```
```

### Usage with recluster (Worker Process)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/cluster-adapter

Defines the worker process for Socket.IO when using the cluster adapter with the 'recluster' module. It initializes Socket.IO, applies the cluster adapter, and sets up the connection with the primary process.

```javascript
const http = require("http");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
const { setupWorker } = require("@socket.io/sticky");  
const { createAdapter } = require("@socket.io/cluster-adapter");  
  
const httpServer = http.createServer();  
const io = new Server(httpServer);  
  
// use the cluster adapter  
io.adapter(createAdapter());  
  
// setup connection with the primary process  
setupWorker(io);  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  /* ... */  
});  

```

### Register and Use Namespace Middleware

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/namespaces

Demonstrates how to register middleware functions for both the default and custom namespaces. Middleware can validate incoming sockets and modify them before connection.

```javascript
// registers a middleware for the default namespace
io.use((socket, next) => {
  if (isValid(socket.request)) {
    next();
  } else {
    next(new Error('invalid'));
  }
});

// registers a middleware for a custom namespace
io.of('/admin').use(async (socket, next) => {
  const user = await fetchUser(socket.handshake.query);
  if (user.isAdmin) {
    socket.user = user;
    next();
  } else {
    next(new Error('forbidden'));
  }
});
```

### Returns

### Request Example

// Or pass no server and then call the method

```
```

### Add Custom Query Parameters to Socket.IO Handshake (Client & Server)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Shows how to append custom query parameters to the Socket.IO handshake request from the client, which are then accessible on the server via `socket.handshake.query`. These parameters cannot be updated after the initial connection.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";  

const socket = io({  
  query: {  
    x: 42  
  }  
});  
```

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  console.log(socket.handshake.query); // prints { x: "42", EIO: "4", transport: "polling" }  
});  
```

```javascript
socket.io.on("reconnect_attempt", () => {  
  socket.io.opts.query.x++;  
});  
```

### Manager Connection Management

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-api

Methods for opening, connecting, and creating sockets within a manager.

```APIDOC
## Manager Connection Management

### `manager.open([callback])`

* `callback` _(Function)_
* **Returns** `Manager`

If the manager was initiated with `autoConnect` to `false`, launch a new connection attempt. The `callback` argument is optional and will be called once the attempt fails/succeeds.

### `manager.connect([callback])`

Synonym of `manager.open([callback])`.

### `manager.socket(nsp, options)`

* `nsp` _(String)_
* `options` _(Object)_
* **Returns** `Socket`

Creates a new `Socket` for the given namespace. Only `auth` (`{ auth: {key: "value"} }`) is read from the `options` object. Other keys will be ignored and should be passed when instancing a `new Manager(nsp, options)`.
```

### Socket.IO Emitter SocketsJoin Utility

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/adapter

Demonstrates the `socketsJoin()` utility method provided by the Socket.IO emitter, allowing you to programmatically make sockets join specified rooms, either globally or within a specific namespace.

```javascript
// make all Socket instances join the "room1" room
emitter.socketsJoin("room1");

// make all Socket instances of the "admin" namespace in the "room1" room join the "room2" room
emitter.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsJoin("room2");
```

### Initialize Socket.IO Client (Custom Namespace)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-initialization

Establishes a connection to a specific Socket.IO namespace. This can be done either from the same origin or from a different domain by appending the namespace to the URL.

```javascript
// same origin version  
const socket = io('/admin');  
```

```javascript
// cross origin version  
const socket = io('https://server-domain.com/admin');  
```

Initializes the Socket.IO client when the front-end is served from the same domain as the server. The server URL is automatically deduced from the `window.location` object.

const socket = io();

### Initialize Socket.IO Client from Different Domain

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Illustrates initializing the Socket.IO client to connect to a server hosted on a different domain. The server's URL must be explicitly provided, and Cross-Origin Resource Sharing (CORS) must be enabled on the server. Supports `https` and `wss` protocols.

```javascript
const socket = io("https://server-domain.com");  
const socket = io("wss://server-domain.com");  
const socket = io("server-domain.com"); // only in the browser when the page is served over https (will not work in Node.js)  
```

### Managing Socket Rooms

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-socket-instance

Shows how to access and manage the rooms a Socket instance is currently part of. A new Socket initially joins a room named after its own ID. You can use `socket.join()` to add the socket to other rooms.

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log(socket.rooms); // Set { <socket.id> }
  socket.join("room1");
  console.log(socket.rooms); // Set { <socket.id>, "room1" }
});
```

--------------------------------

### Securing a Namespace with Middleware (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/namespaces

Demonstrates how to use middleware to control access to a specific Socket.IO namespace, '/admin' in this case. The middleware checks for sufficient user rights before allowing a connection and subsequent event handling.

```javascript
const adminNamespace = io.of("/admin");

adminNamespace.use((socket, next) => {
  // ensure the user has sufficient rights
  next();
});

adminNamespace.on("connection", socket => {
  socket.on("delete user", () => {
    // ...
  });
});
```

--------------------------------

### Sequential Middleware Execution

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/namespaces

Illustrates how multiple middleware functions registered for the same namespace are executed sequentially. If any middleware calls `next` with an error, subsequent middleware and the connection process are halted.

```javascript
io.use((socket, next) => {
  next();
});

io.use((socket, next) => {
  next(new Error('thou shall not pass'));
});

io.use((socket, next) => {
  // not executed, since the previous middleware has returned an error
  next();
});
```

--------------------------------

### Basic Emit to Single Client (Server)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/emit-cheatsheet

Shows how to emit a basic event named 'hello' with various data types to a single connected client from the server. The data can include numbers, strings, JSON objects, and Buffers.

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  socket.emit("hello", 1, "2", { 3: "4", 5: Buffer.from([6]) });  
});  
```

io.on("connection", (socket) => {

  console.log(socket.rooms); // Set { <socket.id>, "room1" }

### fetchSockets

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Retrieves matching Socket instances based on various criteria. It can fetch all sockets, sockets within a specific room, sockets in a room of a specific namespace, or sockets associated with a single socket ID.

```APIDOC
## GET /websites/socket_io/fetchSockets

### Description
Retrieves matching Socket instances.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/websites/socket_io/fetchSockets

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **namespace** (string) - Optional - The namespace to fetch sockets from. Defaults to the main namespace.
- **room** (string) - Optional - The room to filter sockets by. If not provided, all sockets in the namespace are returned.
- **socketId** (string) - Optional - A specific socket ID to fetch.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Fetch all sockets in the main namespace
const sockets = await io.fetchSockets();

// Fetch all sockets in the 'room1' room of the main namespace
const sockets = await io.in("room1").fetchSockets();

// Fetch all sockets in the 'room1' room of the 'admin' namespace
const sockets = await io.of("/admin").in("room1").fetchSockets();

// Fetch a specific socket by its ID
const sockets = await io.in(theSocketId).fetchSockets();
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **sockets** (array) - An array of Socket objects, each exposing properties like `id`, `handshake`, `rooms`, and `data`. These objects also have methods like `emit`, `join`, `leave`, and `disconnect`.

#### Response Example
```json
[
  {
    "id": "socket_id_1",
    "handshake": { /* ... */ },
    "rooms": ["room1", "room2"],
    "data": { "username": "alice" }
  },
  {
    "id": "socket_id_2",
    "handshake": { /* ... */ },
    "rooms": ["room1"],
    "data": {}
  }
]
```
```

--------------------------------

### Connect to Socket.IO with Query Option Object (Client)

Demonstrates passing query parameters as an object within the connection options. This method is an alternative to including them directly in the URL.

  query: {  
    token: 'cde'  
  }

```

### Accessing Underlying Engine and Events

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-socket-instance

Shows how to access the underlying `Manager` and its `engine` property via `socket.io`. It demonstrates listening to various engine events like transport upgrades, packet transmission, and connection closure.

```javascript
socket.on("connect", () => {
  const engine = socket.io.engine;
  console.log(engine.transport.name); // in most cases, prints "polling"

  engine.once("upgrade", () => {
    // called when the transport is upgraded (i.e. from HTTP long-polling to WebSocket)
    console.log(engine.transport.name); // in most cases, prints "websocket"
  });

  engine.on("packet", ({ type, data }) => {
    // called for each packet received
  });

  engine.on("packetCreate", ({ type, data }) => {
    // called for each packet sent
  });

  engine.on("drain", () => {
    // called when the write buffer is drained
  });

  engine.on("close", (reason) => {
    // called when the underlying connection is closed
  });
});
```

### Namespace Properties

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Provides information about the namespace's name, connected sockets, and adapter.

```APIDOC
## Namespace Properties

### Description
Represents a pool of sockets connected under a given scope identified by a pathname (e.g., `/chat`). Properties provide access to the namespace identifier, connected sockets, and the adapter used.

### Properties
* **name** (String) - The namespace identifier property (e.g., `/admin`).
* **sockets** (Map <SocketId, Socket>) - A map of Socket instances currently connected to this namespace on the current node.
* **adapter** (Adapter) - The "Adapter" used for the namespace. Useful for managing sockets and rooms across a cluster, especially with adapters like Redis.

### Request Example
```javascript
// Get the number of sockets in the '/admin' namespace
const socketCount = io.of("/admin").sockets.size;

// Access the adapter for the default namespace
const defaultNamespaceAdapter = io.of("/").adapter;
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
* **Namespace Properties** - Access to `name`, `sockets`, and `adapter`.

#### Response Example
```json
// Properties are accessed directly on the Namespace object, not returned as JSON.
// Example: io.of('/someNamespace').name would return '/someNamespace'
```
```

### Register All Event Handlers in Index File with Socket.IO 3.x

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-application-structure

Illustrates consolidating all Socket.IO event handler registrations within the main index.js file. Handler logic is imported from separate modules (e.g., orderHandler.js, userHandler.js) and then attached to the socket within the connection handler.

```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer);

const { createOrder, readOrder } = require("./orderHandler")(io);
const { updatePassword } = require("./userHandler")(io);

const onConnection = (socket) => {
  socket.on("order:create", createOrder);
  socket.on("order:read", readOrder);

  socket.on("user:update-password", updatePassword);
}

io.on("connection", onConnection);

```

```javascript
module.exports = (io) => {
  const createOrder = function (payload) {
    const socket = this; // hence the 'function' above, as an arrow function will not work
    // ...
  };

  const readOrder = function (orderId, callback) {
    // ...
  };

  return {
    createOrder,
    readOrder
  }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Sequential Execution of Socket.IO Middlewares

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/middlewares

Illustrates the sequential execution of multiple middleware functions in Socket.IO. Each middleware must call the `next()` function to pass control to the subsequent one. If any middleware returns an error using `next(error)`, the chain is broken, and the connection is refused.

```javascript
io.use((socket, next) => {
  next();
});

io.use((socket, next) => {
  next(new Error("thou shall not pass"));
});

io.use((socket, next) => {
  // not executed, since the previous middleware has returned an error
  next();
});
```

--------------------------------

### Include Extra Headers in Socket.IO Handshake (Client & Server)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Demonstrates adding custom headers to the Socket.IO handshake request from the client, accessible on the server via `socket.handshake.headers`. Note that this feature may not work with WebSocket-only connections in browsers.

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";  

const socket = io({  
  extraHeaders: {  
    "my-custom-header": "1234"  
  }  
});  
```

```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  console.log(socket.handshake.headers); // an object containing "my-custom-header": "1234"  
});  
```

```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";  

const socket = io({  
  transports: ["websocket"],  
  extraHeaders: {  
    "my-custom-header": "1234" // WARN: this will be ignored in a browser  
  }  
});  
```

```javascript
const socket = io({  
  extraHeaders: {  
    count: 0  
  }  
});  

setInterval(() => {  
  socket.io.opts.extraHeaders.count++;  
}, 1000);  
```

### Sharded Adapter Configuration

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Configuration options for the sharded Socket.IO Redis adapter.

```APIDOC
## Sharded Adapter Configuration

### Description
Configuration options for the sharded Socket.IO Redis adapter.

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **channelPrefix** (string) - Optional - The prefix for the Redis Pub/Sub channels. Defaults to `socket.io`.
- **subscriptionMode** (string) - Optional - The subscription mode impacts the number of Redis Pub/Sub channels used by the adapter. Defaults to `dynamic`.
  - **Available values for `subscriptionMode`**:
    - `static`: 2 Pub/Sub channels per namespace. Useful for dynamic namespaces.
    - `dynamic`: (2 + 1 per public room) per namespace. Useful when some rooms have a low number of clients.
    - `dynamic-private`: (2 + 1 per room) per namespace. Like `dynamic` but creates separate channels for private rooms. Useful for 1:1 communication via `socket.emit()`.
```

### Update Socket.IO Client Authentication on Connection Error

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Illustrates how to dynamically update authentication credentials when a Socket.IO connection fails with an error. This allows for re-authentication with new credentials.

```javascript
socket.on("connect_error", (err) => {
  if (err.message === "invalid credentials") {
    socket.auth.token = "efgh";
    socket.connect();
  }
});
```

### Initialize Socket.IO with MongoDB adapter (TTL Index)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/mongo-adapter

Initializes a Socket.IO server and configures the MongoDB adapter using a TTL index for automatic document expiration. This method requires adding a `createdAt` field to documents.

```typescript
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/mongo-adapter";  
import { MongoClient } from "mongodb";  
  
const DB = "mydb";  
const COLLECTION = "socket.io-adapter-events";  
  
const io = new Server();  
  
const mongoClient = new MongoClient("mongodb://localhost:27017/?replicaSet=rs0");  
  
await mongoClient.connect();  
  
const mongoCollection = mongoClient.db(DB).collection(COLLECTION);  
  
await mongoCollection.createIndex(  
  { createdAt: 1 },  
  { expireAfterSeconds: 3600, background: true }  
);  
  
io.adapter(createAdapter(mongoCollection, {  
  addCreatedAtField: true  
}));  
  
io.listen(3000);  

```

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Describes the `onconnection` method, which is an advanced feature for manually creating Socket.IO clients from incoming engine.io sockets. This allows for custom socket handling logic when integrating Socket.IO with non-standard transport layers.

```javascript
// socket: An engine.IO Socket instance.
// Returns the Socket.IO Server instance.
io.onconnection(socket);
```

### Integrate Socket.IO Admin UI with Node.js Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/admin-ui

This code snippet demonstrates how to integrate the Socket.IO admin UI into a Node.js server. It requires creating an HTTP server, initializing a Socket.IO server, and then calling the `instrument` function with the Socket.IO instance.

```javascript
const { createServer } = require("http");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
const { instrument } = require("@socket.io/admin-ui");  
  
const httpServer = createServer();  
  
const io = new Server(httpServer, {  
  cors: {  
    origin: ["https://admin.socket.io"],  
    credentials: true  
  }  
});  
  
instrument(io, {  
  auth: false,  
  mode: "development",  
});  
  
httpServer.listen(3000);
```

### Socket Instance Additional Attributes and Middlewares

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-socket-instance

Learn how to attach custom attributes to a Socket instance and implement server-side middlewares for processing incoming packets.

```APIDOC
## Socket Instance Additional Attributes and Middlewares

### Additional Attributes

As long as you do not overwrite any existing attribute, you can attach any attribute to the Socket instance and use it later.

**Example:**
```javascript
// in a middleware
io.use(async (socket, next) => {
  try {
    const user = await fetchUser(socket);
    socket.user = user;
  } catch (e) {
    next(new Error("unknown user"));
  }
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log(socket.user);

  // in a listener
  socket.on("set username", (username) => {
    socket.username = username;
  });
});
```

### Socket Middlewares

These middlewares are called for each incoming packet. They can be used for logging, authorization, rate limiting, etc.

**Usage:**
```javascript
socket.use(([event, ...args], next) => {
  // do something with the packet (logging, authorization, rate limiting...)
  // do not forget to call next() at the end
  next();
});
```

**Error Handling in Middlewares:**
The `next` method can be called with an error object. This will prevent the event from reaching registered handlers and emit an `error` event instead.

**Example:**
```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.use(([event, ...args], next) => {
    if (isUnauthorized(event)) {
      return next(new Error("unauthorized event"));
    }
    next();
  });

  socket.on("error", (err) => {
    if (err && err.message === "unauthorized event") {
      socket.disconnect();
    }
  });
});
```

*Note: This feature only exists on the server-side. For the client-side, consider using catch-all listeners.*
```

--------------------------------

### Set Custom Socket.IO Path

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Demonstrates how to set a custom path for serving Socket.IO and engine.io resources. The `path()` method allows specifying a unique URL path, which must also be configured on the client-side for connection.

```javascript
const io = require('socket.io')();
io.path('/myownpath');

// client-side
const socket = io({
  path: '/myownpath'
});
```

### Create and Manage Namespaces using server.of(nsp)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Initializes and retrieves a Namespace by its identifier. Supports string, RegExp, or function for dynamic namespace creation, enabling fine-grained control over client connections and events.

```javascript
const adminNamespace = io.of("/admin");  

const dynamicNsp = io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/).on("connection", (socket) => {  
  const newNamespace = socket.nsp; // newNamespace.name === "/dynamic-101"  
  
  // broadcast to all clients in the given sub-namespace  
  newNamespace.emit("hello");  
});  

// client-side  
const socket = io("/dynamic-101");  

// broadcast to all clients in each sub-namespace  
dynamicNsp.emit("hello");  

// use a middleware for each sub-namespace  
dynamicNsp.use((socket, next) => { /* ... */ });  

io.of((name, query, next) => {  
  // the checkToken method must return a boolean, indicating whether the client is able to connect or not.  
  next(null, checkToken(query.token));  
}).on("connection", (socket) => { /* ... */ });  
```

--------------------------------

### Initialize Socket.IO Server with CommonJS

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-initialization

Initializes a Socket.IO server using CommonJS syntax by attaching it to an HTTP server. This method is common for Node.js projects not using ES modules.

```javascript
const httpServer = require("http").createServer();
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  // ...
});

io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});

httpServer.listen(3000);

```

### Manager Constructor

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-api

Instantiates a new Socket.IO Manager to manage connections to a server.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io

### Description
Instantiates a new Socket.IO Manager to manage connections to a server.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/websites/socket_io

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **url** (String) - The URL of the Socket.IO server.
- **options** (Object) - Optional configuration object.
  - **path** (String) - Default: `/socket.io/ - Name of the path that is captured on the server side.
  - **reconnection** (Boolean) - Default: `true` - Whether to reconnect automatically.
  - **reconnectionAttempts** (Number) - Default: `Infinity` - Number of reconnection attempts before giving up.
  - **reconnectionDelay** (Number) - Default: `1000` - Initial delay before attempting a new reconnection (affected by `randomizationFactor`).
  - **reconnectionDelayMax** (Number) - Default: `5000` - Maximum time to wait between reconnections.
  - **randomizationFactor** (Number) - Default: `0.5` - Randomization factor for reconnection delay (0 <= randomizationFactor <= 1).
  - **timeout** (Number) - Default: `20000` - Connection timeout before an `error` event is emitted.
  - **autoConnect** (Boolean) - Default: `true` - Automatically connect when the manager is instantiated. If false, `manager.open()` must be called manually.
  - **query** (Object) - Default: `{}` - Additional query parameters sent when connecting a namespace.
  - **parser** (Object) - The parser to use. Defaults to the built-in `Parser`.
  - **upgrade** (Boolean) - Default: `true` - Whether the client should try to upgrade the transport.
  - **forceJSONP** (Boolean) - Default: `false` - Forces JSONP for polling transport.
  - **jsonp** (Boolean) - Default: `true` - Determines whether to use JSONP when necessary for polling.
  - **forceBase64** (Boolean) - Default: `false` - Forces base 64 encoding for polling transport.
  - **enablesXDR** (Boolean) - Default: `false` - Enables XDomainRequest for IE8.
  - **timestampRequests** (Boolean) - Whether to add a timestamp with each transport request. Polling requests are always stamped unless explicitly set to `false`.
  - **timestampParam** (String) - Default: `t` - The timestamp parameter name.
  - **transports** (Array<String>) - Default: `["polling", "websocket"]` - A list of transports to try in order.
  - **transportOptions** (Object) - Hash of options, indexed by transport name, overriding common options.
  - **rememberUpgrade** (Boolean) - Default: `false` - If true, attempts to bypass the normal upgrade process if the previous websocket connection succeeded.
  - **onlyBinaryUpgrades** (Boolean) - Default: `false` - Restricts transport upgrades to transports supporting binary data.
  - **requestTimeout** (Number) - Default: `0` - Timeout for xhr-polling requests in milliseconds (only for polling transport).
  - **protocols** (Array) - A list of subprotocols (only for websocket transport).
  - **agent** (Object) - Node.js-only: The `http.Agent` to use.
  - **pfx** (Object) - Node.js-only: Certificate, Private key, and CA certificates for SSL.
  - **key** (Object) - Node.js-only: Private key for SSL.
  - **passphrase** (String) - Node.js-only: Passphrase for the private key or pfx.
  - **cert** (Object) - Node.js-only: Public x509 certificate for SSL.
  - **ca** (Object) - Node.js-only: Authority certificate(s) for SSL verification.
  - **ciphers** (String) - Node.js-only: String describing the ciphers to use or exclude.
  - **rejectUnauthorized** (Boolean) - Default: `true` - Node.js-only: Verifies the server certificate against supplied CAs.
  - **perMessageDeflate** (Boolean) - Node.js-only: Parameters of the WebSocket permessage-deflate extension. Set to `false` to disable.
  - **extraHeaders** (Object) - Node.js-only: Headers passed for each request to the server.
  - **forceNode** (Boolean) - Node.js-only: Default: `false` - Uses NodeJS implementation for websockets even if a native browser implementation is available.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "url": "http://localhost:3000",
  "options": {
    "reconnection": false,
    "timeout": 10000,
    "query": {
      "token": "abcdef12345"
    }
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **Manager** (Object) - An instance of the Manager.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "message": "Manager created successfully"
}
```
```

--------------------------------

### Creating a Custom Namespace (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/namespaces

Shows the server-side method for creating and configuring a custom Socket.IO namespace using `io.of()`.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io");

// Create a custom namespace named '/my-namespace'
const nsp = io.of("/my-namespace");

nsp.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log("Someone connected to /my-namespace");

  // Emit a message to all clients connected to this namespace
  nsp.emit("hi", "everyone!");

  socket.on("disconnect", () => {
    console.log("User disconnected from /my-namespace");
  });
});
```

--------------------------------

### Client Initialization - Same Domain

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-initialization

Initializes the Socket.IO client connecting to the server on the same domain. The server URL is automatically deduced from the window.location object.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io

### Description
Initializes the Socket.IO client connecting to the server on the same domain. The server URL is automatically deduced from the window.location object.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **path** (string) - Optional - The path to the Socket.IO server, defaults to '/socket.io'.
- **reconnection** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to attempt reconnection, defaults to true.
- **reconnectionAttempts** (number) - Optional - Maximum number of reconnection attempts, defaults to Infinity.
- **reconnectionDelay** (number) - Optional - Initial delay between reconnection attempts in milliseconds, defaults to 1000.
- **reconnectionDelayMax** (number) - Optional - Maximum delay between reconnection attempts in milliseconds, defaults to 5000.
- **randomizationFactor** (number) - Optional - Factor for randomizing reconnection delay, defaults to 0.5.
- **timeout** (number) - Optional - Connection timeout in milliseconds, defaults to 20000.
- **autoConnect** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to automatically connect, defaults to true.
- **query** (object) - Optional - Custom query parameters to send with the connection.
- **upgrade** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to attempt upgrading the transport, defaults to true.
- **forceJSONP** (boolean) - Optional - Force using JSONP transport, defaults to false.
- **jsonp** (boolean) - Optional - Enable JSONP transport, defaults to true.
- **forceBase64** (boolean) - Optional - Force using Base64 encoding for data, defaults to false.
- **enablesXDR** (boolean) - Optional - Enable XDR for cross-domain requests, defaults to false.
- **timestampRequests** (boolean) - Optional - Append a timestamp to requests to prevent caching, defaults to true.
- **timestampParam** (string) - Optional - Parameter name for the timestamp, defaults to 't'.
- **policyPort** (number) - Optional - Port for Flash policy file requests, defaults to 843.
- **transports** (array of strings) - Optional - List of allowed transports, defaults to ['polling', 'websocket'].
- **transportOptions** (object) - Optional - Options for specific transports.
- **rememberUpgrade** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to remember the upgrade transport for subsequent connections, defaults to false.
- **onlyBinaryUpgrades** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to only upgrade to binary-supporting transports, defaults to false.
- **requestTimeout** (number) - Optional - Timeout for individual requests in milliseconds, defaults to 0.
- **protocols** (array of strings) - Optional - List of protocols to support.
- **agent** (boolean or object) - Optional - Node.js http agent options.
- **pfx** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL PFX certificate.
- **key** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL key.
- **passphrase** (string) - Optional - Node.js SSL passphrase.
- **cert** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL certificate.
- **ca** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL CA certificate.
- **ciphers** (array of strings) - Optional - Node.js SSL ciphers.
- **rejectUnauthorized** (boolean) - Optional - Node.js SSL reject unauthorized option, defaults to true.
- **perMessageDeflate** (boolean or object) - Optional - Node.js WebSocket per-message deflate option.
- **forceNode** (boolean) - Optional - Force Node.js transport, defaults to false.
- **localAddress** (string) - Optional - Node.js local address.
- **extraHeaders** (object) - Optional - Additional headers for Node.js requests.

### Request Example
```javascript

```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)

```

### Connection Event

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Handles new incoming connections. The 'connection' event is emitted when a new client successfully connects to the Socket.IO server.

```APIDOC
## Events

### `connection`

### Description
This event is fired upon a new connection from a client.

### Method
Server Event

### Parameters
- **socket** (Socket) - Required - An instance of the Socket object representing the connected client.

### Event Example
```javascript
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log("A user connected with ID:", socket.id);

  // Example: Handling custom events from the client
  socket.on("chat message", (msg) => {
    console.log("Message from client:", msg);
    io.emit("chat message", msg); // Broadcast message to all clients
  });

  // Example: Handling disconnection
  socket.on("disconnect", () => {
    console.log("User disconnected:", socket.id);
  });
});
```

### Configure Client Serving in Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Demonstrates how to control whether Socket.IO serves client files. The `serveClient(false)` method can be called before `attach` to disable client file serving. Alternatively, the `serveClient` option can be passed during initialization with an http server.

```javascript
// pass a server and the `serveClient` option
const io = require("socket.io")(http, { serveClient: false });

// or pass no server and then you can call the method
const io = require("socket.io")();
io.serveClient(false);
io.attach(http);
```

--------------------------------

### Leave Rooms with Socket.IO SocketsLeave

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Illustrates how to make Socket instances leave specified rooms. This functionality mirrors `socketsJoin` in terms of targeting specific sockets or groups of sockets.

```javascript
// make all Socket instances leave the "room1" room
io.socketsLeave("room1");

// make all Socket instances in the "room1" room leave the "room2" and "room3" rooms
io.in("room1").socketsLeave(["room2", "room3"]);

// make all Socket instances in the "room1" room of the "admin" namespace leave the "room2" room
io.of("/admin").in("room1").socketsLeave("room2");

// this also works with a single socket ID
io.in(theSocketId).socketsLeave("room1");
```

```javascript
io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/);
```

### Serve HTML with Express (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-2

This snippet shows how to set up an Express server to serve an `index.html` file. It uses CommonJS module syntax and the `res.sendFile` method to handle the request for the root path. Dependencies include `express` and Node.js built-in modules for HTTP and path manipulation.

```javascript
const express = require('express');
const { createServer } = require('node:http');
const { join } = require('node:path');

const app = express();
const server = createServer(app);

app.get('/', (req, res) => {
  res.sendFile(join(__dirname, 'index.html'));
});

server.listen(3000, () => {
  console.log('server running at http://localhost:3000');
});

```

io.use((socket, next) => {

});

});

### Socket Middlewares for Packet Processing

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-socket-instance

Explains how to implement server-side middlewares for the Socket instance that execute for every incoming packet. These middlewares can be used for tasks like logging, authorization, or rate limiting before the packet reaches its intended event handler. The `next()` function must be called to proceed.

```javascript
socket.use(([event, ...args], next) => {
  // do something with the packet (logging, authorization, rate limiting...)
  // do not forget to call next() at the end
  next();
});
```

### WebSocket Upgrade Process

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/engine-io-protocol

Details the steps required for a client to upgrade an Engine.IO connection from HTTP long-polling to WebSocket and the expected server responses.

```APIDOC
## WebSocket Upgrade Process

### Description
This section describes the procedure for upgrading an Engine.IO connection to WebSocket. The client first establishes an HTTP long-polling connection and then initiates an upgrade to WebSocket if available.

### Client Actions
1. Pause the HTTP long-polling transport to prevent packet loss.
2. Open a WebSocket connection using the same session ID.
3. Send a `ping` packet with the payload `probe`.
4. Upon receiving a `pong` packet with the payload `probe`, send an `upgrade` packet to finalize the process.

### Server Actions
1. Send a `noop` packet to any pending HTTP GET requests to close the long-polling transport gracefully.
2. Respond to the client's `ping` packet with a `pong` packet containing the payload `probe`.

### Sequence Diagram
```
CLIENT                                                 SERVER  
  
  │                                                      │  
  │   GET /engine.io/?EIO=4&transport=websocket&sid=...  │  
  │ ───────────────────────────────────────────────────► │  
  │  ◄─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │  
  │            HTTP 101 (WebSocket handshake)            │  
  │                                                      │  
  │            -----  WebSocket frames -----             │  
  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────►  │  
  │                         2probe                       │ (ping packet)  
  │  ◄─────────────────────────────────────────────────  │  
  │                         3probe                       │ (pong packet)  
  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────►  │  
  │                         5                            │ (upgrade packet)  
  │                                                      │  
```
```

--------------------------------

```

### Handle HTTP Upgrades with Socket.IO and WebSocket Servers

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Demonstrates how to use `engine.handleUpgrade` to manage HTTP upgrade requests, allowing both a Socket.IO server and a plain WebSocket server to coexist. This method is crucial for handling incoming connections that are not standard HTTP requests.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http";  
import { Server as WsServer } from "ws";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
  
const httpServer = createServer();  
const wss = new WsServer({ noServer: true });  
const io = new Server(httpServer);  
  
httpServer.removeAllListeners("upgrade");  
  
httpServer.on("upgrade", (req, socket, head) => {  
  if (req.url === "/") {  
    wss.handleUpgrade(req, socket, head, (ws) => {  
      wss.emit("connection", ws, req);  
    });  
  } else if (req.url.startsWith("/socket.io/")) {  
    io.engine.handleUpgrade(req, socket, head);  
  } else {  
    socket.destroy();  
  }  
});  
  
httpServer.listen(3000);  

```

--------------------------------

### Socket.IO Server with HTTPS (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-initialization

Sets up a Socket.IO server using Node.js HTTPS module and CommonJS module system. Requires SSL certificate and key files for secure connection. Listens for incoming connections on port 3000.

```javascript
const { readFileSync } = require("fs");  
const { createServer } = require("https");  
const { Server } = require("socket.io");  
  
const httpsServer = createServer({
  key: readFileSync("/path/to/my/key.pem"),  
  cert: readFileSync("/path/to/my/cert.pem")  
});  
  
const io = new Server(httpsServer, { /* options */ });  
  
io.on("connection", (socket) => {  
  // ...  
});  
  
httpsServer.listen(3000);  


```

### Artillery Socket.IO v3 Load Testing Scenario

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/load-testing

A sample Artillery scenario file (YAML) for load testing a Socket.IO server. It defines target server, load phases, uses the socketio-v3 engine, and specifies a flow including emitting messages, using namespaces, and handling acknowledgments.

```yaml
# my-scenario.yml  
config:  
  target: "http://localhost:3000"  
  phases:  
    - duration: 60  
      arrivalRate: 10  
  engines:  
   socketio-v3: {}  
  
scenarios:  
  - name: My sample scenario  
    engine: socketio-v3  
    flow:  
      # wait for the WebSocket upgrade (optional)  
      - think: 1  
  
      # basic emit  
      - emit:  
          channel: "hello"  
          data: "world"  
  
      # emit an object  
      - emit:  
          channel: "hello"  
          data:  
            id: 42  
            status: "in progress"  
            tags:  
              - "tag1"  
              - "tag2"  
  
      # emit in a custom namespace  
      - namespace: "/my-namespace"  
        emit:  
          channel: "hello"  
          data: "world"  
  
      # emit with acknowledgement  
      - emit:  
          channel: "ping"  
        acknowledge:  
          match:  
            value: "pong"  
  
      # do nothing for 30 seconds then disconnect  
      - think: 30  

```

### Configure Node.js Client for Client Certificate Authentication (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Establish a secure Socket.IO connection from a Node.js client that presents its own certificate to the server for authentication. This involves providing the server's CA certificate, the client's certificate, and the client's private key in the connection options.

```javascript
const fs = require("fs");
const socket = require("socket.io-client")("https://example.com", {

### Socket.IO with Redis Adapter (redis package)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/redis-adapter

Configures a Socket.IO server to use the Redis adapter with the 'redis' package. This allows for inter-server communication in a Socket.IO cluster. It requires connecting both a publisher and subscriber client to Redis.

```javascript
import { createClient } from "redis";  
import { Server } from "socket.io";  
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-adapter";  
  
const pubClient = createClient({ url: "redis://localhost:6379" });  
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();  
  
await Promise.all([  
  pubClient.connect(),  
  subClient.connect()  
]);  
  
const io = new Server({  
  adapter: createAdapter(pubClient, subClient)  
});  
  
io.listen(3000);  


```

--------------------------------

### Emit Events with MongoDB Emitter (Node.js)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/mongo-adapter

This snippet demonstrates how to connect to MongoDB, initialize the mongo-emitter, and emit 'ping' events every second. It requires 'mongodb' and '@socket.io/mongo-emitter' packages. The input is a MongoDB connection string and a collection name, and the output is 'ping' events emitted to the specified MongoDB collection.

```javascript
const { Emitter } = require("@socket.io/mongo-emitter");  
const { MongoClient } = require("mongodb");  
  
const mongoClient = new MongoClient("mongodb://localhost:27017/?replicaSet=rs0");  
  
const main = async () => {  
  await mongoClient.connect();  
  
  const mongoCollection = mongoClient.db("mydb").collection("socket.io-adapter-events");  
  const emitter = new Emitter(mongoCollection);  
  
  setInterval(() => {  
    emitter.emit("ping", new Date());  
  }, 1000);  
}  
  
main();  

```

const adminNamespace = io.of('/admin');

for (const socket of sockets) {
  console.log(socket.id);
  console.log(socket.handshake);
  console.log(socket.rooms);
  console.log(socket.data);
  socket.emit(/* ... */);
  socket.join(/* ... */);
  socket.leave(/* ... */);
  socket.disconnect(/* ... */);
}
```

io.to('some room').emit('some event');

io.to('room1').to('room2').to('room3').emit('some event');

## Adapter Configuration

### Description
Sets the adapter for communication between Socket.IO server instances. Defaults to a memory-based adapter.

### Method
`server.adapter([value])`

### Parameters
- **value** (Adapter) - An instance of an adapter (e.g., `socket.io-redis`).

### Returns
`Server|Adapter` - The server instance or the current adapter if no argument is supplied.

### Request Example

const io = require('socket.io')(3000);

### Initialize Socket.IO with Existing HTTP/2 Server (CommonJS)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-initialization

Attaches a Socket.IO server to an existing Node.js HTTP/2 server using CommonJS. It requires SSL certificates and allows HTTP/1 connections.

```javascript
const fs = require("fs");
const httpServer = require("http2").createSecureServer({
  allowHTTP1: true,
  key: fs.readFileSync("/tmp/key.pem"),
  cert: fs.readFileSync("/tmp/cert.pem")
});
const options = { /* ... */ };
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, options);

io.on("connection", socket => { /* ... */ });

httpServer.listen(3000);
```

### Socket.IO Emitter Basic Usage

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/adapter

Illustrates the basic usage of the Socket.IO emitter for broadcasting events to all clients or specific rooms across multiple Socket.IO servers. This is essential for inter-process communication in scaled applications.

```javascript
// to all clients
emitter.emit(/* ... */);

// to all clients in "room1"
emitter.to("room1").emit(/* ... */);

// to all clients in "room1" except those in "room2"
emitter.to("room1").except("room2").emit(/* ... */);

const adminEmitter = emitter.of("/admin");

// to all clients in the "admin" namespace
adminEmitter.emit(/* ... */);

// to all clients in the "admin" namespace and in the "room1" room
adminEmitter.to("room1").emit(/* ... */);
```

--------------------------------

### Managing Rooms in Custom Namespaces (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/namespaces

Illustrates how to manage rooms within Socket.IO namespaces. Sockets can join rooms, and messages can be broadcast to specific rooms within a namespace, enabling targeted communication.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io");

const orderNamespace = io.of("/orders");

orderNamespace.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log("Socket connected to orders namespace");
  socket.join("room1"); // Join a room named "room1"
  console.log(`Socket ${socket.id} joined room1 in orders namespace`);

  // Emit a message to all sockets in "room1" within the "orders" namespace
  orderNamespace.to("room1").emit("hello", "Message for room1 in orders");
});

const userNamespace = io.of("/users");

userNamespace.on("connection", (socket) => {
  console.log("Socket connected to users namespace");
  socket.join("room1"); // Join a room named "room1" (distinct from the one in "orders")
  console.log(`Socket ${socket.id} joined room1 in users namespace`);

  // Emit a message to all sockets in "room1" within the "users" namespace
  userNamespace.to("room1").emit("holà", "Message for room1 in users");
});
```

### Integrate Passport with Express Session for Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/namespaces

Shows how to combine `express-session` and `Passport` middleware with Socket.IO using the `wrap` function, facilitating authentication for Socket.IO connections.

```javascript
const session = require('express-session');
const passport = require('passport');

io.use(wrap(session({ secret: 'cats' })));
io.use(wrap(passport.initialize()));
io.use(wrap(passport.session()));

io.use((socket, next) => {
  if (socket.request.user) {
    next();
  } else {
    next(new Error('unauthorized'))
  }
});
```

### Customize Socket.IO Server Path (Server-side)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-options

Demonstrates how to configure a custom path for the Socket.IO server. This is useful when running Socket.IO behind a proxy or when you need to avoid conflicts with other WebSocket services. The `path` option must match on both the server and client.

```javascript
import { createServer } from "http";
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const httpServer = createServer();
const io = new Server(httpServer, {
  path: "/my-custom-path/"
});
```

### Configure Node.js Server with Self-Signed Certificate (JavaScript)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-initialization

Create a Node.js HTTPS server secured with a self-signed certificate for Socket.IO communication. This requires reading the certificate and key files using the `fs` module and passing them to `https.createServer` along with other TLS options.

```javascript
const fs = require("fs");
const server = require("https").createServer({
  cert: fs.readFileSync("./cert.pem"),
  key: fs.readFileSync("./key.pem")
});
const io = require("socket.io")(server);
```

### Sharing Data Between Socket.IO Servers

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-instance

Demonstrates using the `data` attribute on Socket instances to share arbitrary information across different Socket.IO servers in a cluster. Data is set on one server and can be accessed on another.

```javascript
// server A
io.on("connection", (socket) => {
  socket.data.username = "alice";
});

// server B
const sockets = await io.fetchSockets();
console.log(sockets[0].data.username); // "alice"
```

### Configure Swift Socket.IO Client with EIO Version 3

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/troubleshooting-connection-issues

This Swift code demonstrates how to configure a Socket.IO client to connect to a server using protocol version 3, even if the client library defaults to a newer version. This is achieved by setting specific connection parameters, namely `EIO` to `3`. This is useful for maintaining backward compatibility with older Socket.IO servers.

```swift
SocketManager(socketURL: URL(string:"http://localhost:8087/")!, config: [.connectParams(["EIO": "3"])])
```

### Configure Socket.IO with a Custom Parser

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-api

This snippet illustrates how to integrate a custom parser, such as 'socket.io-msgpack-parser' or 'socket.io-json-parser', with Socket.IO for improved performance. Both the client and server must use the same custom parser to ensure communication compatibility.

```javascript
const parser = require('socket.io-msgpack-parser'); // or require('socket.io-json-parser')
const socket = io({
  parser: parser
});

// the server-side must have the same parser, to be able to communicate
const io = require('socket.io')({
  parser: parser
});
```

### Socket Connection Management

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/client-api

Methods for manually connecting, opening, and sending data via a socket.

```APIDOC
## Socket Connection Management

### `socket.connect()`

Added in v1.0.0
* **Returns** `Socket`

Manually connects the socket.
```javascript
const socket = io({
  autoConnect: false
});

// ...
socket.connect();
```
It can also be used to manually reconnect:
```javascript
socket.on("disconnect", () => {
  socket.connect();
});
```

### `socket.open()`

Added in v1.0.0
Synonym of `socket.connect()`.

### `socket.send([...args][, ack])`

* `args`
* `ack` _(Function)_
* **Returns** `Socket`

Sends a `message` event. See `socket.emit(eventName[, ...args][, ack])`.
```

### Wrapper for Express Middleware Compatibility

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/namespaces

Provides a utility function `wrap` to adapt Express middleware to the Socket.IO middleware signature, allowing the use of existing Express middleware with Socket.IO.

```javascript
const wrap = middleware => (socket, next) => middleware(socket.request, {}, next);
```

### Implement Custom Request Authorization

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-initialization

This snippet demonstrates how to use the 'allowRequest' option to implement custom logic for authorizing incoming requests. A callback function is provided, which receives the request object and must call back with an error or a boolean indicating authorization status.

```javascript
const io = require("socket.io")(httpServer, {
  allowRequest: (req, callback) => {
    const isOriginValid = check(req);
    callback(null, isOriginValid);
  }
});

```

### socket.prependAny(callback)

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Registers a 'catch-all' listener at the beginning of the listener array. This ensures the listener is executed before other 'onAny' listeners.

```APIDOC
## socket.prependAny(callback)

### Description
Registers a new catch-all listener. The listener is added to the beginning of the listeners array, ensuring it's executed before other `onAny` listeners.

### Method
`socket.prependAny(callback)`

### Endpoint
N/A (Socket method)

### Parameters
- **callback** (Function) - The function to execute for any event. It receives the event name as the first argument, followed by any additional arguments sent with the event.

### Request Example
```javascript
socket.prependAny((event, ...args) => {
  console.log(`PREPENDED: Received event: ${event} with arguments:`, args);
});
```

### Response
#### Success Response (N/A)
N/A

#### Response Example
N/A
```

### Create Socket.IO Namespace

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v3/server-api

Initializes and retrieves a Socket.IO Namespace by its pathname identifier. Namespaces allow for logical separation of sockets. Supports string, RegExp, or Function for dynamic namespace creation.

```javascript
const adminNamespace = io.of("/admin");

const dynamicNsp = io.of(/^\/dynamic-\d+$/).on("connection", (socket) => {
  const newNamespace = socket.nsp; // newNamespace.name === "/dynamic-101"
  newNamespace.emit("hello");
});

io.of((name, query, next) => {
  next(null, checkToken(query.token));
}).on("connection", (socket) => {
  // ...
});
```

### Client Initialization - Different Domain

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-initialization

Initializes the Socket.IO client connecting to a server on a different domain. Requires specifying the server URL.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/socket_io

### Description
Initializes the Socket.IO client connecting to a server on a different domain. Requires specifying the server URL.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **url** (string) - Required - The URL of the Socket.IO server (e.g., 'https://server-domain.com').
- **path** (string) - Optional - The path to the Socket.IO server, defaults to '/socket.io'.
- **reconnection** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to attempt reconnection, defaults to true.
- **reconnectionAttempts** (number) - Optional - Maximum number of reconnection attempts, defaults to Infinity.
- **reconnectionDelay** (number) - Optional - Initial delay between reconnection attempts in milliseconds, defaults to 1000.
- **reconnectionDelayMax** (number) - Optional - Maximum delay between reconnection attempts in milliseconds, defaults to 5000.
- **randomizationFactor** (number) - Optional - Factor for randomizing reconnection delay, defaults to 0.5.
- **timeout** (number) - Optional - Connection timeout in milliseconds, defaults to 20000.
- **autoConnect** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to automatically connect, defaults to true.
- **query** (object) - Optional - Custom query parameters to send with the connection.
- **upgrade** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to attempt upgrading the transport, defaults to true.
- **forceJSONP** (boolean) - Optional - Force using JSONP transport, defaults to false.
- **jsonp** (boolean) - Optional - Enable JSONP transport, defaults to true.
- **forceBase64** (boolean) - Optional - Force using Base64 encoding for data, defaults to false.
- **enablesXDR** (boolean) - Optional - Enable XDR for cross-domain requests, defaults to false.
- **timestampRequests** (boolean) - Optional - Append a timestamp to requests to prevent caching, defaults to true.
- **timestampParam** (string) - Optional - Parameter name for the timestamp, defaults to 't'.
- **policyPort** (number) - Optional - Port for Flash policy file requests, defaults to 843.
- **transports** (array of strings) - Optional - List of allowed transports, defaults to ['polling', 'websocket'].
- **transportOptions** (object) - Optional - Options for specific transports.
- **rememberUpgrade** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to remember the upgrade transport for subsequent connections, defaults to false.
- **onlyBinaryUpgrades** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to only upgrade to binary-supporting transports, defaults to false.
- **requestTimeout** (number) - Optional - Timeout for individual requests in milliseconds, defaults to 0.
- **protocols** (array of strings) - Optional - List of protocols to support.
- **agent** (boolean or object) - Optional - Node.js http agent options.
- **pfx** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL PFX certificate.
- **key** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL key.
- **passphrase** (string) - Optional - Node.js SSL passphrase.
- **cert** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL certificate.
- **ca** (string or buffer) - Optional - Node.js SSL CA certificate.
- **ciphers** (array of strings) - Optional - Node.js SSL ciphers.
- **rejectUnauthorized** (boolean) - Optional - Node.js SSL reject unauthorized option, defaults to true.
- **perMessageDeflate** (boolean or object) - Optional - Node.js WebSocket per-message deflate option.
- **forceNode** (boolean) - Optional - Force Node.js transport, defaults to false.
- **localAddress** (string) - Optional - Node.js local address.
- **extraHeaders** (object) - Optional - Additional headers for Node.js requests.

### Request Example
```javascript
const socket = io('https://server-domain.com');
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **socket** (object) - The connected Socket.IO client instance.
```

### Basic HTML Structure for Socket.IO Chat

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/step-2

This is a foundational HTML file structure for a Socket.IO chat application. It includes basic CSS for styling the chat interface, a list element to display messages (`#messages`), and a form with an input field and submit button for sending messages.

```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html>
  <head>
    <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1.0">
    <title>Socket.IO chat</title>
    <style>
      body { margin: 0; padding-bottom: 3rem; font-family: -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, "Segoe UI", Roboto, Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif; }

      #form { background: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.15); padding: 0.25rem; position: fixed; bottom: 0; left: 0; right: 0; display: flex; height: 3rem; box-sizing: border-box; backdrop-filter: blur(10px); }
      #input { border: none; padding: 0 1rem; flex-grow: 1; border-radius: 2rem; margin: 0.25rem; }
      #input:focus { outline: none; }
      #form > button { background: #333; border: none; padding: 0 1rem; margin: 0.25rem; border-radius: 3px; outline: none; color: #fff; }

      #messages { list-style-type: none; margin: 0; padding: 0; }
      #messages > li { padding: 0.5rem 1rem; }
      #messages > li:nth-child(odd) { background: #efefef; }
    </style>
  </head>
  <body>
    <ul id="messages"></ul>
    <form id="form" action="">
      <input id="input" autocomplete="off" /><button>Send</button>
    </form>
  </body>
</html>

```

### Configure Socket.IO with Redis Adapter

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Sets a Redis-based adapter for the Socket.IO server, enabling communication across multiple server instances. This requires the '@socket.io/redis-adapter' and 'redis' packages. It demonstrates connecting to Redis and applying the adapter.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";
import { createAdapter } from "@socket.io/redis-adapter";
import { createClient } from "redis";

const io = new Server();

const pubClient = createClient({ host: "localhost", port: 6379 });
const subClient = pubClient.duplicate();

io.adapter(createAdapter(pubClient, subClient));

// redis@3
io.listen(3000);

// redis@4
Promise.all([pubClient.connect(), subClient.connect()]).then(() => {
  io.listen(3000);
});

```

--------------------------------

### Client Configuration: Advanced Options

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options

This section details advanced client-side configuration options including `rememberUpgrade`, `timestampParam`, `timestampRequests`, and `transportOptions` for fine-tuning connection behavior.

```APIDOC
## Client Configuration: Advanced Options

### Description
This covers several advanced client-side configuration options for Socket.IO, including settings for connection upgrades, timestamp parameters, and transport-specific configurations.

### Method
N/A (Client-side configuration option)

### Endpoint
N/A

### Parameters
#### Client Options
- **rememberUpgrade** (boolean) - Optional - If true, and a previous WebSocket connection succeeded, the client will attempt WebSocket first, bypassing the usual upgrade process. Defaults to `false`. Recommended for SSL/TLS or trusted networks.
- **timestampParam** (string) - Optional - The name of the query parameter used for timestamping (cache-busting). Defaults to `"t"`.
- **timestampRequests** (boolean) - Optional - Whether to include the timestamp query parameter in requests for cache-busting. Defaults to `true`.
- **transportOptions** (object) - Optional - An object containing configuration options specific to each transport (e.g., `websocket`, `polling`). Added in v2.0.0. Defaults to `{}`.

### Request Example for `transportOptions`
```javascript
import { io } from "socket.io-client";

const socket = io({
  path: "/path-for-http-long-polling/",
  transportOptions: {
    websocket: {
      path: "/path-for-websocket/"
    }
  }
});
```

### Response
N/A (These are client-side configuration options)

### Notes
- `rememberUpgrade` should be used with caution.
- `timestampRequests` is enabled by default for cache-busting.
```

--------------------------------

### Join Multiple Rooms with Socket.IO

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v2/server-api

Adds the client to a list of rooms specified in an array. Optionally calls a callback and allows broadcasting to multiple rooms.

```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.join(['room 237', 'room 238'], () => {
    const rooms = Object.keys(socket.rooms);
    console.log(rooms); // [ <socket.id>, 'room 237', 'room 238' ]
    io.to('room 237').to('room 238').emit('a new user has joined the room'); // broadcast to everyone in both rooms
  });
});
```

### Socket.IO Emit with Multiple Arguments and Binary Data - Server

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/tutorial/api-overview

Shows the server receiving multiple arguments, including a Buffer (Node.js specific), sent from the client. It logs each received argument.

```javascript
io.on('connection', (socket) => {
  socket.on('hello', (arg1, arg2, arg3) => {
    console.log(arg1); // 1
    console.log(arg2); // '2'
    console.log(arg3); // { 3: '4', 5: <Buffer 06> }
  });
});
```

--------------------------------

### namespace.fetchSockets()

Source: https://socket.io/docs/v4/server-api

Returns the matching Socket instances within a namespace. Can be filtered by room or socket ID.

```APIDOC
## GET /namespaces/{namespaceId}/sockets

### Description
Returns the matching Socket instances within a namespace. Can be filtered by room or socket ID.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
`/namespaces/{namespaceId}/sockets`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **namespaceId** (string) - Required - The ID of the namespace.

#### Query Parameters
- **roomId** (string) - Optional - Filters sockets belonging to a specific room.
- **socketId** (string) - Optional - Filters for a specific socket ID.

### Request Body
(Not applicable)

### Request Example
```javascript
// Get all sockets in the main namespace
const sockets = await io.fetchSockets();

// Get all sockets in 'room1' of the main namespace
const sockets = await io.in("room1").fetchSockets();

// Get all sockets in 'room1' of the 'admin' namespace
const sockets = await io.of("/admin").in("room1").fetchSockets();

// Get a specific socket by ID
const sockets = await io.in(theSocketId).fetchSockets();
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **array** - An array of socket objects, each containing `id`, `handshake`, `rooms`, and `data`.

#### Response Example
```json
[
  {
    "id": "socket1_id",
    "handshake": { /* ... */ },
    "rooms": ["room1", "room2"],
    "data": { "username": "alice" }
  },
  {
    "id": "socket2_id",
    "handshake": { /* ... */ },
    "rooms": ["room2"],
    "data": {}
  }
]
```

#### Error Response
- **object** - Contains an error message if the operation fails.
```

Demonstrates how to configure the Socket.IO server to use a custom parser. This involves passing the custom parser object to the Server constructor.

```javascript
import { Server } from "socket.io";

const io = new Server({
  parser: myParser
});
```
